features arising simultaneously from decentralized learning in stochastic games: multiple interacting
stochastic iterates, auxiliary games that are not exactly zero-sum during learning, and
time-inhomogeneous Markovian noise generated by time-varying policies. This framework may
be useful for analyzing other learning algorithms with coupled stochastic
iterates.

Two directions remain particularly important as future work. First, focusing on analyzing natural best-response-type algorithms, the current bounds are unlikely to be
optimal, and improving the dependence on
\epsilon^{-1}
, the discount factor, and the
mixing parameters is worth further investigation. Second, the present analyses only focus on the tabular setting, and
extending the algorithmic and analytical framework to the function approximation setting would be necessary for large-scale real-world applications.

Appendices

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
2.4

The proof is divided into
4
steps. In Appendix
A.1
, we prove an important boundedness property for the iterates generated by Algorithm
1
. In Appendices
A.2
and
A.3
, we analyze the evolution of the policies and the
q
-functions by establishing negative drift inequalities with respect to their associated Lyapunov functions. In Appendix
A.4
, we solve the coupled Lyapunov drift inequalities to prove Theorem
2.4
. The proofs of Corollary
2.5
and Corollary
2.6
are presented in Appendices
A.5
and
A.6
, respectively. The statements and proofs of all supporting lemmas used in this section are presented in Appendix
A.7
.

A.1  Boundedness of the Iterates

The following lemma presents the boundedness property.

Lemma A.1
.

It holds for all
k\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
that
\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\min_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\pi_{k}^{i}(a^{i})\geq\ell_{\tau}
,
where
\ell_{\tau}=[(m-1)\exp(2/\tau)+1]^{-1}
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.
We prove the result by induction. Since
q_{0}^{i}=0
and
\pi_{0}^{i}
is initialized as the uniform distribution on
\mathcal{A}^{i}
, the base case holds. Suppose that the result holds for some
k\geq 0
. By Line 5 of Algorithm
1
, for any
a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}
,

\displaystyle|q_{k+1}^{i}(a^{i})|=\,

\displaystyle\left|(1-\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{a^{i}=A_{k}^{i}\}})q_{k}^{i}(a^{i})+\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{a^{i}=A_{k}^{i}\}}R_{i}(A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\max\left\{|q_{k}^{i}(a^{i})|,\,(1-\alpha_{k})|q_{k}^{i}(a^{i})|+\alpha_{k}|R_{i}(A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 1,

where the last inequality follows from the induction hypothesis
\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and the bound
|R_{i}(a^{i},a^{-i})|\leq 1
. Hence,
\|q_{k+1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.

Next, by Line 3 of Algorithm
1
, for any
a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}
,

\displaystyle\pi_{k+1}^{i}(a^{i})=\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\pi_{k}^{i}(a^{i})+\beta_{k}[\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i})](a^{i})

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\ell_{\tau}+\frac{\beta_{k}}{(m-1)\exp(2\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}/\tau)+1}

(Lemma
A.4
)

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\ell_{\tau}+\beta_{k}\ell_{\tau}

(
\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\ell_{\tau}.

The induction is complete. ∎∎

A.2  Analysis of the Policies

Let
V_{R}:\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})\times\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{2})\to\mathbb{R}
be defined as

\displaystyle V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{\hat{\mu}^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{i}-\mu^{i})^{\top}R_{i}\mu^{-i}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{i})-\tau\nu(\mu^{i})\right\},

(A.1)

where
\nu(\cdot)
is the Shannon entropy. For simplicity of notation, we use
\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
to represent the gradients with respect to the first and second arguments of
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
, respectively. A sequence of properties regarding
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
are provided in Lemma
A.7
.

Next, we present the negative drift inequality of the policies generated by Algorithm
1
with respect to the Lyapunov function
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

Lemma A.2
.

It holds for all
k\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k+1}^{1},\pi_{k+1}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{4}\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

where
L_{\tau}=\tau/\ell_{\tau}+m^{2}/\tau
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.
By Lemma
A.1
, we have
(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau}
for all
k\geq 0
. Using the smoothness property of
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
in Lemma
A.7
(1) and the update equation in Algorithm
1
, Line 3, we have

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k+1}^{1},\pi_{k+1}^{2})\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\pi_{k+1}^{1}-\pi_{k}^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\pi_{k+1}^{2}-\pi_{k}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{\tau}}{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|\pi_{k+1}^{i}-\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{1})-\pi_{k}^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{2})-\pi_{k}^{2}\rangle+\frac{L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}}{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i})-\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\pi_{k}^{2})-\pi_{k}^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\pi_{k}^{1})-\pi_{k}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\pi_{k}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\pi_{k}^{1})\rangle+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2}\right)V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+4\beta_{k}\left(\frac{1}{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{2}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

where the last line follows from Lemma
A.7
(2) and (3), and we used

\sum_{i=1,2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i})-\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4
.

Taking expectations on both sides and using

c_{\alpha,\beta}=\frac{\beta_{k}}{\alpha_{k}}\leq\min\left\{\frac{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{3}}{32},\frac{\ell_{\tau}\tau^{3}}{32m^{2}}\right\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k+1}^{1},\pi_{k+1}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{4}\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}.

This completes the proof. ∎∎

A.3  Analysis of the q-Functions

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
F^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}\times\mathcal{A}^{i}\times\mathcal{A}^{-i}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
be an operator defined as

\displaystyle[F^{i}(q^{i},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})](a^{i})=\mathds{1}_{\{a_{0}^{i}=a^{i}\}}\left(R_{i}(a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})-q^{i}(a_{0}^{i})\right),\quad\forall\,(q^{i},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})\text{ and }a^{i}.

Then, Line
5
of Algorithm
1
can be compactly written as

\displaystyle q_{k+1}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}+\alpha_{k}F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i}).

(A.2)

Given a joint policy
(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})
, let
\bar{F}_{\pi}^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
be defined as

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\pi}^{i}(q^{i}):=\mathbb{E}_{A^{i}\sim\pi^{i}(\cdot),A^{-i}\sim\pi^{-i}(\cdot)}[F^{i}(q^{i},A^{i},A^{-i})]=\text{diag}(\pi^{i})(R_{i}\pi^{-i}-q^{i}).

Then, (
A.2
) can be viewed as a stochastic approximation algorithm for tracking the solution of the time-varying equation
\bar{F}_{\pi_{k}}^{i}(q^{i})=0
. We next present the negative drift inequality of the
q
-functions generated by Algorithm
1
with respect to a norm-square Lyapunov function.

Lemma A.3
.

The following inequality holds for all
k\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\left(1-\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+16\alpha_{k}^{2}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.
For any
k\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, define

\Delta_{k}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i},\qquad\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}.

Since
A_{k}^{i}\sim\pi_{k+1}^{i}(\cdot)
and
A_{k}^{-i}\sim\pi_{k+1}^{-i}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\pi_{k+1}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})=\text{diag}(\pi_{k+1}^{i})(R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}-q_{k}^{i}).

Using the update equation in Line 5 of Algorithm
1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}+\alpha_{k}F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[\langle\bar{F}_{\pi_{k+1}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}),\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\rangle]+4\alpha_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-2\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k})\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+4\alpha_{k}^{2},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
A.1
and

\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]\leq 4
.

Next, by Line 3 of Algorithm
1
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}=\Delta_{k}^{i}+R_{i}(\pi_{k}^{-i}-\pi_{k+1}^{-i})=\Delta_{k}^{i}-\beta_{k}R_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i}).

Using the inequality
\|x+y\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+\eta)\|x\|_{2}^{2}+(1+\eta^{-1})\|y\|_{2}^{2}
with
\eta=\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}/2
, we have

\displaystyle(1-2\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k})\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\|\Delta_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{3\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}\|R_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2},

where we used the stepsize condition
\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}\leq 1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{3m^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+4\alpha_{k}^{2}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}[\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{-i}\pi_{k}^{i})\|_{2}^{2}]+2\mathbb{E}[\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{-i}\pi_{k}^{i})-\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\tau^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{-i}-R_{-i}\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{4}{\tau}\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})].

where we used the
1/\tau
-Lipschitz continuity of
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
and Lemma
A.6
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{6m^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau}\tau^{2}\alpha_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{-i}-R_{-i}\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{12m^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau}\tau\alpha_{k}}\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+4\alpha_{k}^{2}.

Summing over
i=1,2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}+\frac{6m^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau}\tau^{2}\alpha_{k}}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{24m^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau}\tau\alpha_{k}}\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+8\alpha_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+16\alpha_{k}^{2},

where the last line follows from Condition
2.1
. ∎∎

A.4  Solving Coupled Lyapunov Drift Inequalities

For simplicity of notation, denote

\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)=\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]
and

\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)=\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]
. Then, Lemmas
A.2
and
A.3
imply that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2}\right)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{4}\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{2}\right)\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+16\alpha_{k}^{2},\quad\forall\,k\geq 0.

Adding the previous two inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(k+1)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\left(1-\frac{\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}}{4}\right)\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}+16\alpha_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\right)(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(k))+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}+16\alpha_{k}^{2},

(A.3)

where the second inequality follows from
\beta_{k}\leq\ell_{\tau}\alpha_{k}
, or equivalently
c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq\ell_{\tau}
; see Condition
2.1
.

A.4.1  Constant Stepsizes

When using constant stepsizes, i.e.,
\alpha_{k}\equiv\alpha
and
\beta_{k}\equiv\beta
, iterating (
A.3
) gives, for all
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)^{k}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(0)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(0))+8L_{\tau}\beta+\frac{64\alpha^{2}}{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)^{k}(4+2\tau\log(m)+2m)+8L_{\tau}\beta+\frac{64\alpha^{2}}{\beta}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle B_{\text{in}}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{4}\right)^{k}+8L_{\tau}\beta+\frac{64\alpha}{c_{\alpha,\beta}},

where the second inequality follows from
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(0)\leq 4+2\tau\log(m)
and
\mathcal{L}_{q}(0)\leq 2m
. Theorem
2.4
(1) follows by observing that

\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)\geq\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)=\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}_{\tau}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]
.

A.4.2  Diminishing Stepsizes

Consider using
\alpha_{k}=\alpha/(k+h)
and
\beta_{k}=\beta/(k+h)
, where
\beta=c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha
. Recursions of the form (
A.3
) have been well studied in the literature on convergence rates of iterative algorithms
[
35
,
51
,
20
]
. Since
\beta>4
, using the same line of analysis as in
[
20
, Appendix A.2]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}_{\tau}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]=\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)\leq\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)\leq\,

\displaystyle B_{\text{in}}\left(\frac{h}{k+h}\right)^{\beta/4}+\left(64eL_{\tau}\beta+\frac{512e\alpha}{c_{\alpha,\beta}}\right)\frac{1}{k+h}.

A.5  Proof of Corollary
2.5

We use Theorem
2.4
(1) to derive the sample complexity, and choose

\beta=c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha
with
c_{\alpha,\beta}
satisfying Condition
2.1
. To achieve

\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}_{\tau}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\epsilon
, in view of Theorem
2.4
(1), it is sufficient that

\displaystyle B_{\text{in}}e^{-\beta K/4}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3},\quad 8L_{\tau}\beta\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3},\quad\frac{64\alpha}{c_{\alpha,\beta}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

The first inequality is satisfied when
K\geq 4\log(3B_{\text{in}}/\epsilon)/\beta
. Since
\alpha=\beta/c_{\alpha,\beta}
, the last two inequalities are satisfied when

\beta\leq\min\left\{\frac{\epsilon}{24L_{\tau}},\frac{\epsilon c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}}{192}\right\}
. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}_{\tau}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\epsilon
holds as long as

\displaystyle K\geq\frac{96\log(3B_{\text{in}}/\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\max\left\{L_{\tau},\frac{8}{c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}}\right\}.

The result follows by observing that

\max\{L_{\tau},8/c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}\}\leq 8L_{\tau}/c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}
.

A.6  Proof of Corollary
2.6

In view of (
2.3
), for the output
(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})
of Algorithm
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}_{\tau}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]+2\tau\log(m).

Therefore, given
\epsilon>0
, to achieve

\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\epsilon
, it is sufficient that

\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}_{\tau}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\epsilon/2
and

2\tau\log(m)\leq\epsilon/2
. The previous two inequalities hold as long as

\displaystyle\tau\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\log(m)},\quad\text{and}\quad K\geq\frac{3200L_{\tau}\log(3B_{\text{in}}/\epsilon)}{c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}\epsilon},

(A.4)

where the second condition follows from the same line of analysis as in the proof of
Corollary
2.5
.

Choose

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta}=\min\left\{\frac{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{3}}{32},\frac{\ell_{\tau}\tau^{3}}{128m^{2}},\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{L_{\tau}^{1/2}}\right\},

which satisfies Condition
2.1
. Taking

\tau=\epsilon/(4\log(m))
, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{\tau}=\frac{1}{(m-1)\exp(2/\tau)+1}=\frac{1}{(m-1)\exp(8\log(m)/\epsilon)+1}=:f(\epsilon).

Moreover, since
L_{\tau}=\tau/\ell_{\tau}+m^{2}/\tau
, we have

L_{\tau}=\mathcal{O}(1/(\tau\ell_{\tau}))
, where the hidden constant depends only on
m
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{L_{\tau}}{c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\max\left\{\frac{L_{\tau}}{\tau^{2}\ell_{\tau}^{6}},\frac{L_{\tau}}{\ell_{\tau}^{2}\tau^{6}},L_{\tau}^{2}\right\}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\tau^{3}\ell_{\tau}^{7}},\frac{1}{\tau^{7}\ell_{\tau}^{3}}\right\}\right),

where the term
L_{\tau}^{2}
is dominated by the first term because
\tau\leq 1
and

\ell_{\tau}\leq 1
. Substituting
\tau=\epsilon/(4\log(m))
and

\ell_{\tau}=f(\epsilon)
into (
A.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle K=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log(1/\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{4}f(\epsilon)^{3}\min\{f(\epsilon)^{4},\epsilon^{4}\}}\right).

A.7  Statements and Proofs of Supporting Lemmas

Lemma A.4
.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{j\in[d]}[\sigma_{\tau}(x)]_{j}\geq\frac{1}{(d-1)\exp(2\|x\|_{\infty}/\tau)+1}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.4
:
Given any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
j\in[d]
, we have

\displaystyle[\sigma_{\tau}(x)]_{j}=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\exp(x_{j}/\tau)}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\exp(x_{\ell}/\tau)}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{\ell\neq j}\exp((x_{\ell}-x_{j})/\tau)+1}

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)\exp(2\|x\|_{\infty}/\tau)+1}.

The claim follows since the right-hand side is independent of
j
.
∎∎

Lemma A.5
.

For any
i\in\{1,2\}
and any

\mu_{1}^{i},\mu_{2}^{i}\in\{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})\mid\min_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\mu^{i}(a^{i})\geq\ell_{\tau}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\nu(\mu_{1}^{i})-\nabla\nu(\mu_{2}^{i})\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}\|\mu_{1}^{i}-\mu_{2}^{i}\|_{2}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

Fix
i\in\{1,2\}
. For any
\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})
satisfying
\min_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\mu^{i}(a^{i})\geq\ell_{\tau}
, the Hessian of the negative entropy function
\nu(\cdot)
satisfies

\displaystyle 0\cdot I_{m_{i}}\succeq\nabla^{2}\nu(\mu^{i})=-\text{diag}(\mu^{i})^{-1}\succeq\frac{-1}{\min_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\mu^{i}(a^{i})}I_{m_{i}}\succeq\frac{-1}{\ell_{\tau}}I_{m_{i}}.

Therefore,
\nabla\nu(\cdot)
is
1/\ell_{\tau}
-Lipschitz continuous with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}
on the set

\{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})\mid\min_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\mu^{i}(a^{i})\geq\ell_{\tau}\}

[
9
]
. This proves the desired inequality. ∎∎

Lemma A.6
.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have for all
\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})
and
\mu^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})
that

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{i}\mu^{-i})-\mu^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\tau}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}).

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.
Recall that the entropy function
\nu(\cdot)
is
1
-strongly concave with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}

[
27
]
. Fix
i\in\{1,2\}
and
\mu^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})
. Define

\displaystyle F_{i}(\mu^{i}):=\max_{\hat{\mu}^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{i}-\mu^{i})^{\top}R_{i}\mu^{-i}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{i})-\tau\nu(\mu^{i})\right\}.

As a function of
\mu^{i}
,
F_{i}(\mu^{i})
is
\tau
-strongly convex. Moreover, its minimizer is

\sigma_{\tau}(R_{i}\mu^{-i})
, and
F_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{i}\mu^{-i}))=0
. Therefore, by the quadratic growth property of strongly convex functions,

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{i}\mu^{-i})-\mu^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\tau}F_{i}(\mu^{i})\leq\frac{2}{\tau}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}).

This proves the desired result. ∎∎

Denote
\Pi_{\tau}=\{(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})\times\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{2})\mid\min_{a^{1}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}}\pi^{1}(a^{1})\geq\ell_{\tau},\min_{a^{2}\in\mathcal{A}^{2}}\pi^{2}(a^{2})\geq\ell_{\tau}\}
. Note that Lemma
A.1
implies that
(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Lemma A.7
.

The function
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
has the following properties.

1.

The function
V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})
is
L_{\tau}
– smooth on
\Pi_{\tau}
, where

L_{\tau}=\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau}
.

2.

It holds for any
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau}
that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle\leq-V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}).

3.

For any
q^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and
q^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
, we have for all
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau}
that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+4\left(\frac{1}{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{2}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q^{i}-R_{i}\mu^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.7
.
Recall the definition of
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
in (
A.1
). By Danskin’s theorem
[
21
]
and the zero-sum condition
R_{1}+R_{2}^{\top}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\,

\displaystyle-\tau\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})+R_{2}^{\top}\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1}),

(A.5a)

\displaystyle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\,

\displaystyle-\tau\nabla\nu(\mu^{2})+R_{1}^{\top}\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}).

(A.5b)

1.

For any
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau}
, we have by (
A.5
) that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})-\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\|_{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle\tau\|\nabla\nu(\bar{\mu}^{1})-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})\|_{2}+\|R_{2}\|_{2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\bar{\mu}^{1})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}\|\mu^{1}-\bar{\mu}^{1}\|_{2}+\frac{\|R_{2}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau}\|\mu^{1}-\bar{\mu}^{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau}\right)\|\mu^{1}-\bar{\mu}^{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle L_{\tau}\|\mu^{1}-\bar{\mu}^{1}\|_{2}

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.5
and the
1/\tau
– Lipschitz continuity of
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
in
\|\cdot\|_{2}

[
27
]
, and the last inequality follows from
\|R_{i}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{m_{1}m_{2}}\leq m
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})-\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\|_{2}\leq L_{\tau}\|\mu^{2}-\bar{\mu}^{2}\|_{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|\nabla V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})-\nabla V_{R}(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\tau}^{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|\mu^{i}-\bar{\mu}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore,
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
is an
L_{\tau}
– smooth function on
\Pi_{\tau}

[
9
]
.

2.

By the optimality condition of the softmax map, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\right\rangle=0.

Thus, by (
A.5
),

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle=\,

\displaystyle\tau\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}R_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}).

By the concavity of
\nu(\cdot)
and the same optimality condition,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left[(\mu^{1})^{\top}R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\mu^{1})-\max_{\hat{\mu}^{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{1})^{\top}R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{1})\right\}\right].

Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(\mu^{1})^{\top}R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\mu^{1})-\max_{\hat{\mu}^{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{1})^{\top}R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}R_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(\mu^{2})^{\top}R_{2}\mu^{1}+\tau\nu(\mu^{2})-\max_{\hat{\mu}^{2}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{2})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{2})^{\top}R_{2}\mu^{1}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{2})\right\}

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1})^{\top}R_{1}(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}).

Adding the previous two inequalities and using
R_{1}+R_{2}^{\top}=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1})^{\top}(R_{1}+R_{2}^{\top})(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}).

3.

By the optimality condition of the softmax map, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\right\rangle=0.

Therefore, by (
A.5
),

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\tau\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}R_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})).

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the AM-GM inequality, for any
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2c_{1}}\|\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\tau c_{1}}{2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2c_{2}}\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{2}}{2}\|R_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2c_{1}\ell_{\tau}^{2}}\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{2\tau}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2c_{2}}\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{2}\|R_{2}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}\ell_{\tau}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\tau c_{2}}\right)V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{c_{1}}{2\tau}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{2}\|R_{2}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2},

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.5
and the
1/\tau
– Lipschitz continuity of
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
, and the last inequality follows from Lemma
A.6
. Choosing
c_{1}=8/\ell_{\tau}^{2}
and
c_{2}=8/\tau
gives

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{4}{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{2}}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{4\|R_{2}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{4}{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{2}}\|q^{2}-R_{2}\mu^{1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{4\|R_{1}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\|q^{2}-R_{2}\mu^{1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Summing up the previous two inequalities and using
\|R_{i}\|_{2}\leq m
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+4\left(\frac{1}{\tau\ell_{\tau}^{2}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q^{i}-R_{i}\mu^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}.

∎∎

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
2.7

The proof of Theorem
2.7
follows the same high-level structure as that of Theorem
2.4
: we first establish boundedness of the iterates, then derive Lyapunov drift inequalities for the policies and the
q
-functions, and finally solve the resulting coupled inequalities. However, because Algorithm
2
replaces
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
with
\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(\cdot)
, the drift inequalities for both the policies and the
q
-functions differ substantially from those in the proof of Theorem
2.4
.

B.1  Boundedness of the Iterates

Lemma B.1
.

It holds for all
k\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
that
\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\min_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\pi_{k}^{i}(a^{i})\geq\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
, where

\displaystyle\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{m}+\frac{(1-\bar{\epsilon})}{(m-1)\exp(2/\tau)+1}.

The proof of Lemma
B.1
is identical to that of Lemma
A.1
, and therefore is omitted.

B.2  Analysis of the Policies

We also use the Lyapunov function
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
defined in (
A.5
) to analyze the policies. Lemma
B.4
provides the properties of
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
needed for the proof of Theorem
2.7
. We next present the negative drift inequality for the policies generated by Algorithm
2
.

Lemma B.2
.

The following inequality holds for all
k\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k+1}^{1},\pi_{k+1}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+\frac{4\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\bigg(\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+8\bar{\epsilon}\beta_{k}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta_{k}^{2},

where
L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau}
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.
Using the smoothness property of
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
in Lemma
B.4
(1) and the update equation in Line 3 of Algorithm
2
, we have for any
k\geq 0
that

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k+1}^{1},\pi_{k+1}^{2})\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{2})-\pi_{k}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{1})-\pi_{k}^{1}\rangle+\frac{L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta_{k}^{2}}{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{i})-\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\pi_{k}^{1})-\pi_{k}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\pi_{k}^{2})-\pi_{k}^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\pi_{k}^{1})\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\pi_{k}^{2})\rangle+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}+8\bar{\epsilon}\beta_{k}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right),

where the last line follows from Lemma
B.4
(2) and (3), and we used

\sum_{i=1,2}\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{i})-\pi_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4
.

Taking expectations on both sides gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k+1}^{1},\pi_{k+1}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}]+8\bar{\epsilon}\beta_{k}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right).

Finally,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(2\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\right)^{1/2}.

Substituting this bound into the previous display gives the desired result.
∎∎

B.3  Analysis of the q-Functions

Similar to the analysis of Algorithm
1
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
, let

F^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}\times\mathcal{A}^{i}\times\mathcal{A}^{-i}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
be defined as

\displaystyle[F^{i}(q^{i},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})](a^{i})=\mathds{1}_{\{a_{0}^{i}=a^{i}\}}\left(R_{i}(a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})-q^{i}(a_{0}^{i})\right),\quad\forall\,(q^{i},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})\text{ and }a^{i}.

Then, Algorithm
2
, Line 5, can be compactly written as

\displaystyle q_{k+1}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}+\alpha_{k}F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i}).

(B.1)

Given a joint policy
\pi=(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})
, let
\bar{F}_{\pi}^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
be defined as

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\pi}^{i}(q^{i})=\mathbb{E}_{A^{i}\sim\pi^{i}(\cdot),A^{-i}\sim\pi^{-i}(\cdot)}[F^{i}(q^{i},A^{i},A^{-i})]=\text{diag}(\pi^{i})(R_{i}\pi^{-i}-q^{i}).

We next establish the negative drift inequality of the
q
-functions generated by Algorithm
2
with respect to the norm-square Lyapunov function.

Lemma B.3
.

The following inequality holds for all
k\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+16\alpha_{k}^{2}+\frac{24m^{2}}{\alpha_{k}\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}\beta_{k}^{2}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.
For any
k\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, define

\Delta_{k}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}
and

\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}
. Since

A_{k}^{i}\sim\pi_{k+1}^{i}(\cdot)
and
A_{k}^{-i}\sim\pi_{k+1}^{-i}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\pi_{k+1}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})=\text{diag}(\pi_{k+1}^{i})(R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}-q_{k}^{i}).

Using (
B.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}+\alpha_{k}F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[\langle\bar{F}_{\pi_{k+1}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}),\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\rangle]+\alpha_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-2\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k})\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+4\alpha_{k}^{2},

where the last line follows from Lemma
B.1
and

\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]\leq 4
.

Next, by Line 3 of Algorithm
2
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}=\Delta_{k}^{i}-\beta_{k}R_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i}).

Using
\|x+y\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+\eta)\|x\|_{2}^{2}+(1+\eta^{-1})\|y\|_{2}^{2}
with

\eta=\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k}/2
, and using

\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle(1-2\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k})\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k})\|\Delta_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{3\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k}}\|R_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k})\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{3\beta_{k}^{2}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\|R_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]+4\alpha_{k}^{2}.

Since both
\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{-i})
and
\pi_{k}^{-i}
are probability vectors, we have

\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|R_{i}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{-i})-\pi_{k}^{-i})\|_{2}^{2}]\leq 4\|R_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4m^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k+1}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k})\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+4\alpha_{k}^{2}+\frac{12m^{2}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha_{k}}\beta_{k}^{2}.

The final result follows by summing the previous inequality over
i\in\{1,2\}
.
∎∎

B.4  Solving Coupled Lyapunov Drift Inequalities

Denote
\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)=\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-R_{i}\pi_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]
and
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)=\mathbb{E}[V_{R}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]
for simplicity of notation. When
\alpha_{k}\equiv\alpha
and
\beta_{k}\equiv\beta
, Lemmas
B.2
and
B.3
state that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha\right)\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+16\alpha^{2}+\frac{24m^{2}}{\alpha\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}\beta^{2},

(B.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\frac{4\beta}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(k)+8\bar{\epsilon}\beta\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}.

(B.3)

Iterating (
B.2
), we have for all
k\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha\right)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{q}(0)+\frac{16\alpha}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\frac{24\beta^{2}m^{2}}{\alpha^{2}\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(k)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha\right)^{k/2}\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(0)+\frac{4\sqrt{\alpha}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}^{1/2}}+\frac{5\beta m}{\alpha\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}.

Substituting this bound into (
B.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+8\bar{\epsilon}\beta\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{4\beta}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\left[\left(1-\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha\right)^{k/2}\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(0)+\frac{4\sqrt{\alpha}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}^{1/2}}+\frac{5\beta m}{\alpha\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}\right].

Iterating the previous inequality, and using
\beta\leq\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha/2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(0)+8\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\beta k(1-\beta)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(0)+\frac{4}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\left[\frac{4\sqrt{\alpha}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}^{1/2}}+\frac{5\beta m}{\alpha\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)^{k}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(0)+8\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\frac{m}{\tau}\right)\beta k\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(0)\right)

\displaystyle+8\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha+4\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\frac{m}{\tau}\right)\left[\frac{4\sqrt{\alpha}}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}^{1/2}}+\frac{5\beta m}{\alpha\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}\right],

where we used
2L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\beta\leq\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\alpha
.

Since
\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\geq\bar{\epsilon}/m
by Lemma
B.1
,
\bar{\epsilon}=\tau
, and
L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=\tau/\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}+m^{2}/\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)^{k}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(0)+8\alpha k\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(0)\right)+16\tau m+\frac{2m}{\tau}\alpha+\frac{32m^{3/2}\sqrt{\alpha}}{\tau^{3/2}}+\frac{40\beta m^{3}}{\alpha\tau^{2}}.

Note that
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(0)\leq 4+2\tau\log(m)
,
\mathcal{L}_{q}(0)\leq 2m
, and
\text{NG}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})\leq\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+2\tau\log(m)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)^{k}\left(4+2\tau\log(m)+8\sqrt{2m}\alpha k\right)

\displaystyle+16\tau m+2\tau\log(m)+\frac{2m}{\tau}\alpha+\frac{32m^{3/2}\sqrt{\alpha}}{\tau^{3/2}}+\frac{40\beta m^{3}}{\alpha\tau^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 20\sqrt{m}k(1-\beta)^{k}+18\tau m+\frac{2m}{\tau}\alpha+\frac{32m^{3/2}\sqrt{\alpha}}{\tau^{3/2}}+\frac{40\beta m^{3}}{\alpha\tau^{2}}.

B.5  Proof of Corollary
2.8

In view of Theorem
2.7
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle 20\sqrt{m}K(1-\beta)^{K}+18\tau m+\frac{2m}{\tau}\alpha+\frac{32m^{3/2}\sqrt{\alpha}}{\tau^{3/2}}+\frac{40\beta m^{3}}{\alpha\tau^{2}}.

Given
\epsilon>0
, choose
\tau=\epsilon/(90m)
,
\alpha=\epsilon^{2}\tau^{3}/(160^{2}m^{3})
, and
\beta=\epsilon\alpha\tau^{2}/(200m^{3})
. Then
18\tau m=\epsilon/5
, and direct substitution gives

\displaystyle\frac{2m}{\tau}\alpha\leq\frac{\epsilon}{5},\qquad\frac{32m^{3/2}\sqrt{\alpha}}{\tau^{3/2}}=\frac{\epsilon}{5},\qquad\frac{40\beta m^{3}}{\alpha\tau^{2}}=\frac{\epsilon}{5}.

It remains to control the transient term. Since
(1-\beta)^{K}\leq e^{-\beta K}
, it is sufficient to ensure
20\sqrt{m}Ke^{-\beta K}\leq\epsilon/5
. This holds whenever

\displaystyle K\geq\frac{2}{\beta}\log\left(\frac{100\sqrt{m}}{\epsilon\beta}\right).

Indeed, for such
K
, we have
\beta K\geq 2\log(100\sqrt{m}/(\epsilon\beta))
, and hence
Ke^{-\beta K}\leq\epsilon/(100\sqrt{m})
. Therefore,
20\sqrt{m}Ke^{-\beta K}\leq\epsilon/5
. Combining the previous bounds gives
\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{K}^{1},\pi_{K}^{2})]\leq\epsilon
.

Finally, substituting
\tau=\epsilon/(90m)
and
\alpha=\epsilon^{2}\tau^{3}/(160^{2}m^{3})
into
\beta=\epsilon\alpha\tau^{2}/(200m^{3})
gives
\beta=\Theta(\epsilon^{8}/m^{11})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle K=\mathcal{O}\left(m^{11}\epsilon^{-8}\log\left(\frac{m}{\epsilon}\right)\right).

B.6  Statements and Proofs of Supporting Lemmas

Let
\Pi_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=\{(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})\times\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{2})\mid\min_{a^{1}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}}\pi^{1}(a^{1})\geq\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}},\min_{a^{2}\in\mathcal{A}^{2}}\pi^{2}(a^{2})\geq\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}\}
. Note that Lemma
B.1
implies that
(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Lemma B.4
.

The function
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
has the following properties.

1.

The function
V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})
is
L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
– smooth on
\Pi_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
, where
L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau}
.

2.

It holds for any
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle\leq-V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}).

3.

For any
q^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and
q^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
, we have for all
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 8\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+\frac{2}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q^{i}-R_{i}\mu^{-i}\|_{2}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.
The proof of Lemma
B.4
(1) and (2) is identical to that of Lemma
A.7
(1) and (2), and therefore is omitted. For Lemma
B.4
(3), recall that by Danskin’s theorem
[
21
]
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\,

\displaystyle-\tau\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})+R_{2}^{\top}\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1}),

\displaystyle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\,

\displaystyle-\tau\nabla\nu(\mu^{2})+R_{1}^{\top}\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}).

By the optimality condition of the softmax operator, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\right\rangle=0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle=\,

\displaystyle\tau\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}R_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})).

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\tau\|\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\|R_{2}\|_{2}\right)\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}.

Since
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
, Lemma
A.5
implies

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\|R_{2}\|_{2}\right)\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}.

Since

\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\leq 2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle\leq 2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\|R_{2}\|_{2}\right)\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}.

Moreover, by Lemma
B.5
and the
1/\tau
-Lipschitz continuity of
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
,

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\bar{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\tau}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}.

Combining the previous two bounds yields

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\|R_{2}\|_{2}\right)\left(2\bar{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\tau}\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 4\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\|R_{2}\|_{2}\right)+\frac{2}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+\|R_{2}\|_{2}\right)\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 4\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+\frac{2}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\|q^{1}-R_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2},

where the last line follows from
\|R_{2}\|_{2}\leq m
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{2}V_{R}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(R_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle 4\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)+\frac{2}{\tau}\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}}+m\right)\|q^{2}-R_{2}\mu^{1}\|_{2}.

The claim follows from adding the previous two inequalities.
∎∎

Lemma B.5
.

Given
i\in\{1,2\}
, for any
q^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
, we have

\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{i})-\sigma_{\tau}(q^{i})\|_{2}\leq 2\bar{\epsilon}
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.
Given
i\in\{1,2\}
, for any
q^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{i})-\sigma_{\tau}(q^{i})\|_{2}^{2}=\bar{\epsilon}^{2}\sum_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\left(\frac{1}{m_{i}}-\sigma_{\tau}(q^{i})(a^{i})\right)^{2}\leq 4\bar{\epsilon}^{2},

where the inequality follows because both
\text{Unif}(\mathcal{A}^{i})
and
\sigma_{\tau}(q^{i})
are probability vectors. It follows that

\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{i})-\sigma_{\tau}(q^{i})\|_{2}\leq 2\bar{\epsilon}
.
∎∎

Appendix C
Details for the Proof of Theorem
3.4

We begin with a summary of notation.

C.1  Notation

1.

Given a pair of matrices
\{X_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}\times m_{-i}}\}_{i\in\{1,2\}}
and a pair of distributions
\{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})\}_{i\in\{1,2\}}
, we define

\displaystyle V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{\hat{\mu}^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{i}-\mu^{i})^{\top}X_{i}\mu^{-i}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{i})-\tau\nu(\mu^{i})\right\},

(C.1)

where
\nu(\cdot)
is the entropy function. Note that
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
is similar to
V_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
defined in Appendix
A.2
for matrix games. However, we do not assume that
X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}=0
.

2.

Given a pair of value functions
v=(v^{1},v^{2})
and a state
s\in\mathcal{S}
, when
X_{i}=\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, we write
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
for
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

3.

For any joint policy
(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})
and state
s
, given
i\in\{1,2\}
, we define

v^{i}_{*,\pi^{-i}}(s)=\max_{\hat{\pi}^{i}}v^{i}_{\hat{\pi}^{i},\pi^{-i}}(s)
,

v^{i}_{\pi^{i},*}(s)=\min_{\hat{\pi}^{-i}}v^{i}_{\pi^{i},\hat{\pi}^{-i}}(s)
,

v^{-i}_{\pi^{-i},*}(s)=\min_{\hat{\pi}^{i}}v^{-i}_{\pi^{-i},\hat{\pi}^{i}}(s)
, and

v^{-i}_{*,\pi^{i}}(s)=\max_{\hat{\pi}^{-i}}v^{-i}_{\hat{\pi}^{-i},\pi^{i}}(s)
.
Note that
v^{1}_{*,\pi^{2}}+v^{2}_{\pi^{2},*}=0
and
v^{1}_{\pi^{1},*}+v^{2}_{*,\pi^{1}}=0
because of the zero-sum structure.

4.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, denote by
v_{*}^{i}
the unique fixed point of the equation
\mathcal{B}^{i}(v^{i})=v^{i}
, where
\mathcal{B}^{i}(\cdot)
is the minimax Bellman operator defined in Section
3.1
. Note that
v_{*}^{1}+v_{*}^{2}=0
.

5.

For any
t,k\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
\bar{q}_{t,k}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}
be defined as
\bar{q}_{t,k}^{i}(s)=\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,k}^{-i}(s)
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
. In addition, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)=\,

\displaystyle\|v_{t}^{1}+v_{t}^{2}\|_{\infty},\quad\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)=\sum_{i=1,2}\|v_{t}^{i}-v_{*}^{i}\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|q_{t,k}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{t,k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{t,k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)=\,

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v_{t},s}(\pi_{t,k}^{1}(s),\pi_{t,k}^{2}(s)).

These will be the Lyapunov functions used in the analysis.

6.

Given
k_{1}\leq k_{2}
, we denote
\beta_{k_{1},k_{2}}=\sum_{k=k_{1}}^{k_{2}}\beta_{k}
and
\alpha_{k_{1},k_{2}}=\sum_{k=k_{1}}^{k_{2}}\alpha_{k}
.

C.2  Proof of Lemma
4.2

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. The proof uses induction arguments.

1.

Fixing
t\geq 0
, we first show by induction that, if
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\|q_{t,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
, then
\|q_{t,k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
k\geq 0
. The base case holds by the assumption
\|q_{t,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
. Suppose that
\|q_{t,k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for some
k\geq 0
. Then, by Algorithm
3
, Line 6, we have for all
(s,a^{i})
that

\displaystyle|q_{t,k+1}^{i}(s,a^{i})|=\,

\displaystyle|q_{t,k}^{i}(s,a^{i})+\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{(s,a^{i})=(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\}}(R_{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})+\gamma v_{t}^{i}(S_{k+1})-q_{t,k}^{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i}))|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{(s,a^{i})=(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\}})|q_{t,k}^{i}(s,a^{i})|

\displaystyle+\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{(s,a^{i})=(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\}}|R_{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})+\gamma v_{t}^{i}(S_{k+1})|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{(s,a^{i})=(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\}})\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{(s,a^{i})=(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\right)

(C.2)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma},

where (
C.2
) follows from the induction hypothesis, the assumption
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
, the bound
\max_{s,a^{i},a^{-i}}|R_{i}(s,a^{i},a^{-i})|\leq 1
, and the stepsize condition
\alpha_{k}\in[0,1]
. The induction is complete, and hence
\|q_{t,k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
k\geq 0
whenever
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\|q_{t,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
.

We next use induction to show that
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\|q_{t,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
t\geq 0
. The initialization ensures that
\|v_{0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\|q_{0,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
. Suppose that
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\|q_{t,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for some
t\geq 0
. Using the update equation for
v_{t+1}^{i}
in Algorithm
3
, Line 8, and the fact that
\|q_{t,k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
k\geq 0
, we have for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
that

\displaystyle|v_{t+1}^{i}(s)|=\left|\sum_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\pi^{i}_{t,K}(a^{i}|s)q_{t,K}^{i}(s,a^{i})\right|\leq\sum_{a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\pi^{i}_{t,K}(a^{i}|s)\|q_{t,K}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}.

Thus,
\|v_{t+1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
. Moreover, Algorithm
3
, Line 9, gives
\|q_{t+1,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}=\|q_{t,K}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
. The induction is complete, and hence
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and
\|q_{t,0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
t\geq 0
.

2.

We first use induction to show that, given
t\geq 0
, if
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{t,0}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
, then
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{t,k}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
for all
k\in\{0,1,\cdots,K\}
. The base case holds by the assumption. Suppose that
\min_{s\in\mathcal{S},a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\pi_{t,k}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
for some
k\geq 0
. Then, by Algorithm
3
, Line 4, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{t,k+1}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)=\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\pi_{t,k}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)+\beta_{k}\sigma_{\tau}(q_{t,k}^{i}(s))(a^{i})

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\ell_{\tau}+\beta_{k}\ell_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\ell_{\tau},

where the inequality follows from the induction hypothesis, Part (1), Lemma
A.4
, and the stepsize condition
\beta_{k}\in[0,1]
. The induction is complete.

We next use induction to show that
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{t,0}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
for all
t\in\{0,1,\cdots,T\}
. Since
\pi_{0,0}^{i}
is initialized as a uniform policy, the base case holds. Suppose that
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{t,0}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
for some
t\geq 0
. Then
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{t,k}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
for all
k\in\{0,1,\cdots,K\}
. Since
\pi_{t+1,0}^{i}=\pi_{t,K}^{i}
by Algorithm
3
, Line 9, we have
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{t+1,0}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
. The induction is complete.

∎

C.3  Proof of Lemma
4.1

Our ultimate goal is to bound the Nash gap

\displaystyle\text{NG}(\pi_{T,K}^{1},\pi_{T,K}^{2})=\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\max_{\pi^{i}}U^{i}(\pi^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i})-U^{i}(\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i})\right)

(C.3)

as a function of the Lyapunov functions.
We first bound the Nash gap using the value functions of the output policies from Algorithm
3
.

Lemma C.1
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\max_{\pi^{i}}U^{i}(\pi^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i})-U^{i}(\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i})\right)\leq\sum_{i=1,2}\left\|v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(C.4)

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.
Using the definition of the utility function, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\max_{\pi^{i}}U^{i}(\pi^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i})-U^{i}(\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i})\right)=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\max_{\pi^{i}}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim p_{o}}\left[v^{i}_{\pi^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(S)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(S)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim p_{o}}\left[\max_{\pi^{i}}v^{i}_{\pi^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(S)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(S)\right]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim p_{o}}\left[v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(S)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(S)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left\|v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}\right\|_{\infty}.

∎∎

The next lemma bounds the right-hand side of (
C.4
) using the iterates from Algorithm
3
.

Lemma C.2
.

It holds for
i\in\{1,2\}
that

\displaystyle\left\|v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)+2\tau\log(m)\right).

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.
For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\,

\displaystyle\left|v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(s)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(s)\right|

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(s)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(s)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},*}(s)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}(s)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},*}(s)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle v^{i}_{*}(s)-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}(s)+v^{-i}_{*}(s)-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},*}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2}\left\|v^{-j}_{*}-v^{-j}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-j},*}\right\|_{\infty}.

Since the right-hand side does not depend on
s
, we have, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}-v^{i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{i},\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{j=1,2}\left\|v^{-j}_{*}-v^{-j}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-j},*}\right\|_{\infty}.

(C.5)

It remains to bound the right-hand side of (
C.5
). For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\,

\displaystyle v^{-i}_{*}(s)-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}(s)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}(s)-v^{i}_{*}(s)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\min_{\mu^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{*}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle+\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\mu^{-i}

\displaystyle+\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\mu^{-i}-\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{*}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}\right|.

(C.6)

We next bound the four terms on the right-hand side of (
C.6
).

For the first term, using the definition of
\mathcal{T}^{i}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\max_{\mu^{i}}\left|(\mu^{i})^{\top}\left(\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}})(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*})(s)\right)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|v^{i}_{*}-v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}\right\|_{\infty}.

Similarly, the second term is bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{*})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)\right|\leq\gamma\left\|v^{i}_{*}-v^{i}_{T}\right\|_{\infty}.

We next consider the third term. We decompose it as

\displaystyle\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\mu^{-i}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{T}^{i})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s))^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{T}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{-i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v_{T}^{-i})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)+\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{-i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{T}^{i})(s)^{\top}\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{j=1,2}\max_{\mu^{j}}(\mu^{j})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{j}(v_{T}^{j})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-j}(s)\right|.

(C.7)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
C.7
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{-i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v_{T}^{-i})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)+\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{-i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{T}^{i})(s)^{\top}\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{-i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v_{T}^{-i})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{-i})^{\top}[-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{T}^{i})(s)]^{\top}\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\max_{\mu^{-i}}\left|(\mu^{-i})^{\top}\left(\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v_{T}^{-i})(s)+\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{T}^{i})(s)^{\top}\right)\pi_{T,K}^{i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|v_{T}^{-i}+v_{T}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
C.7
), using the Lyapunov function
V_{v_{T},s}(\cdot,\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j=1,2}\max_{\mu^{j}}(\mu^{j})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{j}(v_{T}^{j})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-j}(s)\right|=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2}\max_{\mu^{j}}(\mu^{j}-\pi_{T,K}^{j}(s))^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{j}(v_{T}^{j})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-j}(s)

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{j=1,2}(\pi_{T,K}^{j}(s))^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{j}(v_{T}^{j})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-j}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{v_{T},s}(\pi_{T,K}^{1}(s),\pi_{T,K}^{2}(s))+2\tau\log(m)+\gamma\|v_{T}^{1}+v_{T}^{2}\|_{\infty}.

Using the previous two bounds in (
C.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{\mu^{i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\pi_{T,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\mu^{-i}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{v_{T},s}(\pi_{T,K}^{1}(s),\pi_{T,K}^{2}(s))+2\gamma\|v_{T}^{1}+v_{T}^{2}\|_{\infty}+2\tau\log(m).

(C.8)

For the fourth term, using the definition of
\mathcal{T}^{i}(\cdot)
and the Lipschitz property of the matrix-game value, we have

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s)\mu^{-i}-\max_{\mu^{i}}\min_{\mu^{-i}}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{*}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\max_{a^{i},a^{-i}}\left|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{T})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{*}^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\|v_{T}^{i}-v_{*}^{i}\|_{\infty}.

Combining the bounds for the four terms in (
C.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|v^{-i}_{*}-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|v^{i}_{*,\pi_{T,K}^{-i}}-v^{i}_{*}\right\|_{\infty}+2\gamma\|v_{T}^{1}+v_{T}^{2}\|_{\infty}+2\gamma\|v^{i}_{T}-v^{i}_{*}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v_{T},s}(\pi_{T,K}^{1}(s),\pi_{T,K}^{2}(s))+2\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\|v^{-i}_{*}-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}\|_{\infty}+2\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)+2\|v^{i}_{T}-v^{i}_{*}\|_{\infty}+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)+2\tau\log(m).

Rearranging terms gives

\displaystyle\left\|v^{-i}_{*}-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)+2\|v^{i}_{T}-v^{i}_{*}\|_{\infty}+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)+2\tau\log(m)\right).

Summing both sides over
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left\|v^{-i}_{*}-v^{-i}_{\pi_{T,K}^{-i},*}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)+2\tau\log(m)\right).

Using the previous inequality in (
C.5
), we obtain the desired result.
∎∎

The proof of Lemma
4.1
follows by combining Lemmas
C.1
and
C.2
in (
C.3
).

C.4  Analysis of the Outer Loop

C.4.1  Proof of Lemma
4.3

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, using the outer-loop update equation in Algorithm
3
, Line 8, and the fact that
\mathcal{B}^{i}(v_{*}^{i})=v_{*}^{i}
, we have for any
t\geq 0
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
that

\displaystyle v_{t+1}^{i}(s)-v_{*}^{i}(s)=\,

\displaystyle\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-v_{*}^{i}(s)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)-\mathcal{B}^{i}(v_{*}^{i})(s)+\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{B}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s).

Since the minimax Bellman operator
\mathcal{B}^{i}(\cdot)
is a
\gamma
– contraction mapping in
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|v_{t+1}^{i}(s)-v_{*}^{i}(s)\right|\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|v^{i}_{t}-v_{*}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}+\left|\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{B}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\right|.

(C.9)

It remains to bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
C.9
). Using the definition of
\mathcal{B}^{i}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{B}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\right|

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left|\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\min_{\mu^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left|\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\min_{\mu^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}(\mu^{i}-\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s))^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)

\displaystyle+\left|(\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s))^{\top}\left(\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-\max_{\mu^{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{i})}\min_{\mu^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})}(\mu^{i})^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\mu^{-i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right\|_{\infty}+2V_{v_{t},s}(\pi_{t,K}^{1}(s),\pi_{t,K}^{2}(s))

\displaystyle+2\gamma\|v_{t}^{1}+v_{t}^{2}\|_{\infty}+3\tau\log(m),

where the last line follows from the same argument as in (
C.8
), with
T
replaced by
t
. Using the previous inequality in (
C.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|v^{i}_{t+1}-v_{*}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|v^{i}_{t}-v_{*}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}+\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+2\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v_{t},s}(\pi_{t,K}^{1}(s),\pi_{t,K}^{2}(s))+2\gamma\|v_{t}^{1}+v_{t}^{2}\|_{\infty}+3\tau\log(m).

Summing both sides over
i\in\{1,2\}
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)+6\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right\|_{\infty}.

To bound the last term, observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i}_{t})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)-q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right\|_{\infty}=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left\|\bar{q}_{t,K}^{i}-q_{t,K}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left\|\bar{q}_{t,K}^{i}-q_{t,K}^{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(2\sum_{i=1,2}\left\|\bar{q}_{t,K}^{i}-q_{t,K}^{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K).

(C.10)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)+4\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)+6\tau\log(m).

This completes the proof.
∎

C.4.2  Proof of Lemma
4.4

Using the outer-loop update equation in Algorithm
3
, Line 8, we have for any
t\geq 0
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
that

\displaystyle\left|v_{t+1}^{1}(s)+v_{t+1}^{2}(s)\right|=\,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1,2}\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}q_{t,K}^{i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1,2}\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s)^{\top}(q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s))\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{i=1,2}(\pi_{t,K}^{i}(s))^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\max_{(s,a^{i},a^{-i})}\left|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})+\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v_{t}^{-i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)\|_{\infty}+\gamma\|v_{t}^{1}+v_{t}^{2}\|_{\infty},

where the last line follows from the definition of
\mathcal{T}^{i}(\cdot)
. Since the right-hand side does not depend on
s
, we have

\displaystyle\|v_{t+1}^{1}+v_{t+1}^{2}\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\|v_{t}^{1}+v_{t}^{2}\|_{\infty}+\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|q_{t,K}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,K}^{-i}(s)\|_{\infty}.

The result follows from using (
C.10
) to bound the last term on the right-hand side and then using
\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)
and
\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,K)
to simplify the notation.
∎

C.5  Analysis of the Inner Loop

For ease of presentation, we write down only the inner loop of Algorithm
3
in Algorithm
5
, where we omit the outer-loop index
t
. Similarly, we write
\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)
for
\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)
and
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)
for
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)
. All results derived for the
q
-functions and policies of Algorithm
5
can be combined with the outer-loop analysis of Algorithm
3
using a conditioning argument and the Markov property.

Algorithm 5

Inner Loop of Algorithm
3

1:

Input:
Integer
K
, initializations
q_{0}^{i}
and
\pi_{0}^{i}
, and a value function
v^{i}
from the outer loop. Note that
\|q_{0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
,
\|v^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
, and
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{0}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\ell_{\tau}
by Lemma
4.2
.

2:

for

k=0,1,\cdots,K-1

do

3:

\pi_{k+1}^{i}(s)=\pi_{k}^{i}(s)+\beta_{k}(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{i}(s))
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}

4:

Sample
A_{k}^{i}\sim\pi_{k+1}^{i}(\cdot\mid S_{k})
, receive reward
R_{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})
, and observe
S_{k+1}\sim p(\cdot\mid S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})

5:

q_{k+1}^{i}(s,a^{i})=q_{k}^{i}(s,a^{i})+\alpha_{k}\mathds{1}_{\{(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})=(s,a^{i})\}}\left(R_{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})+\gamma v^{i}(S_{k+1})-q_{k}^{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\right)
for all
(s,a^{i})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}^{i}

6:

end

for

C.5.1  Proof of Lemma
4.5

To begin with, by Danskin’s theorem
[
21
]
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})=\,

\displaystyle-(X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top})\mu^{2}-\tau\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})+X_{2}^{\top}\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1}).

(C.11)

A similar result holds for
\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})
.

1.

It is clear that the function
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
is non-negative. The strong convexity follows from the following two observations.

(a)

The negative entropy
-\nu(\cdot)
is
1
– strongly convex with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}

[
9
, Example 5.27]
.

(b)

Given
i\in\{1,2\}
, the function

\max_{\hat{\mu}^{-i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{-i})}\{(\hat{\mu}^{-i})^{\top}X_{-i}\mu^{i}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{-i})\}
,
as a function of
\mu^{i}
, is the maximum of linear functions in
\mu^{i}
, and therefore is convex.

It follows that, for any
i\in\{1,2\}
, the function
V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})
is
\tau
– strongly convex in
\mu^{i}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}
, uniformly over
\mu^{-i}
.

2.

For any
(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\in\Pi_{\tau}
, we have by (
C.11
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})-\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left\|(X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top})(\mu^{2}-\bar{\mu}^{2})+\tau(\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})-\nabla\nu(\bar{\mu}^{1}))+X_{2}^{\top}(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\bar{\mu}^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1}))\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}\|\mu^{2}-\bar{\mu}^{2}\|_{2}+\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\|X_{2}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau}\right)\left\|\bar{\mu}^{1}-\mu^{1}\right\|_{2},

(C.12)

where (
C.12
) follows from Lemma
A.5
and the Lipschitz continuity of
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)

[
27
]
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})-\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\right\|_{2}\leq\|X_{2}+X_{1}^{\top}\|_{2}\|\mu^{1}-\bar{\mu}^{1}\|_{2}+\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\|X_{1}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau}\right)\left\|\bar{\mu}^{2}-\mu^{2}\right\|_{2}.

Using the previous two inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})-\nabla V_{X}(\bar{\mu}^{1},\bar{\mu}^{2})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\left[2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\|X_{-i}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau}\right)^{2}\left\|\bar{\mu}^{i}-\mu^{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\|X_{i}+X_{-i}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}\|\mu^{-i}-\bar{\mu}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2}^{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}\right)^{2}+\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\sum_{i=1,2}\|\bar{\mu}^{i}-\mu^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore,
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
is an
\tilde{L}_{\tau}
– smooth function on
\Pi_{\tau}

[
9
]
, where

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{\tau}=2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2}^{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}+\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}\right).

3.

By the optimality condition of the softmax map, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle X_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\right\rangle=0.

Using (
C.11
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle=\,

\displaystyle\tau\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}X_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}).

By the concavity of
\nu(\cdot)
and the same optimality condition,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left[(\mu^{1})^{\top}X_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\mu^{1})-\max_{\hat{\mu}^{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{1})^{\top}X_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{1})\right\}\right].

Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle(\mu^{1})^{\top}X_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\mu^{1})-\max_{\hat{\mu}^{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{1})^{\top}X_{1}\mu^{2}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{1})\right\}

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}X_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle(\mu^{2})^{\top}X_{2}\mu^{1}+\tau\nu(\mu^{2})-\max_{\hat{\mu}^{2}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{2})}\left\{(\hat{\mu}^{2})^{\top}X_{2}\mu^{1}+\tau\nu(\hat{\mu}^{2})\right\}

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1})^{\top}X_{1}(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}).

Adding the previous two inequalities gives

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1})^{\top}(X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top})(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+2\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\|_{2}\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2},

(C.13)

where the last line follows from
\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\leq\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})\|_{1}+\|\mu^{2}\|_{1}\leq 2
.
Using Part (1) together with the quadratic growth property of strongly convex functions, we have

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\tau}}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{1/2}.

It follows from (
C.13
) that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\tau}}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{1/2}\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-\frac{7}{8}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{16}{\tau}\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}.

4.

For any
u^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
, using (
C.11
) and the optimality condition of the softmax map in inner-product form, we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(u^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\tau\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}(u^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}X_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}(u^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\tau\|\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\|X_{2}\|_{2}\right)\|\sigma_{\tau}(u^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})-\mu^{1}\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\|X_{2}\|_{2}\right)\frac{1}{\tau}\|u^{1}-X_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\tau}}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\|X_{2}\|_{2}}{\tau}\right)V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{1/2}\|u^{1}-X_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}
