\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{8}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\|X_{2}\|_{2}}{\tau}\right)^{2}\|u^{1}-X_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}^{2}.

Similarly, for any
u^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(u^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{8}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\|X_{1}\|_{2}}{\tau}\right)^{2}\|u^{2}-X_{2}\mu^{1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Adding the previous two inequalities gives

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(u^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(u^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+\frac{8}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2})}{\tau}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|u^{i}-X_{i}\mu^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}.

∎

C.5.2  Proof of Lemma
4.6

We will use
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
(see Appendix
C.1
) as the Lyapunov function to study the evolution of
(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))
.
To begin with, we identify the smoothness parameter of
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Using Lemma
4.5
(1) and the definition of
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{\tau}=\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2}^{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}+\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\max(\|\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)\|_{2}^{2},\|\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}+\|\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)+\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)^{\top}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{\tau}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{2m}{1-\gamma}\right):=L_{\tau},

where the inequality follows from
|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})|\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
(s,a^{i},a^{-i})
and
i\in\{1,2\}
. Therefore,
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
is an
L_{\tau}
– smooth function on
\Pi_{\tau}
.

Using the smoothness of
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
, we have by the policy update equation in Algorithm
5
, Line 3, that

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k+1}^{1}(s),\pi_{k+1}^{2}(s))

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{2}(s))-\pi_{k}^{2}(s)\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{1}(s))-\pi_{k}^{1}(s)\rangle+\frac{L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}}{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)\pi_{k}^{1}(s))-\pi_{k}^{2}(s)\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)\pi_{k}^{2}(s))-\pi_{k}^{1}(s)\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{2}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)\pi_{k}^{1}(s))\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{1}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)\pi_{k}^{2}(s))\rangle+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\frac{16\beta_{k}}{\tau}\|\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)+\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{8\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}}{\tau}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

where the last line follows from Lemma
4.5
(3) and (4).

Since
\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}\leq\frac{m}{1-\gamma}
and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)+\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}\leq m^{2}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}^{2},

we have

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k+1}^{1}(s),\pi_{k+1}^{2}(s))

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\frac{16\beta_{k}m^{2}}{\tau}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{8\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m}{\tau(1-\gamma)}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\frac{16\beta_{k}m^{2}}{\tau}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{8\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m}{\tau(1-\gamma)}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|q_{k}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\frac{16\beta_{k}m^{2}}{\tau}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{8\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m}{\tau(1-\gamma)}\right)^{2}\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}.

Since the right-hand side does not depend on
s
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\frac{16\beta_{k}m^{2}}{\tau}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{8\beta_{k}}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\ell_{\tau}}+\frac{m}{\tau(1-\gamma)}\right)^{2}\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)+\frac{16\beta_{k}m^{2}}{\tau}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{32m^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau^{3}\ell_{\tau}^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

where the last line follows from
\tau\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
.
∎

C.5.3  Proof of Lemma
4.8

1.

For any
(q_{1}^{i},q_{2}^{i})
and
(s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\|F^{i}(q_{1}^{i},s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})-F^{i}(q_{2}^{i},s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{(s,a^{i})}\left([F^{i}(q_{1}^{i},s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})](s,a^{i})-[F^{i}(q_{2}^{i},s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})](s,a^{i})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left(q_{1}^{i}(s_{0},a_{0}^{i})-q_{2}^{i}(s_{0},a_{0}^{i})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\|q_{1}^{i}-q_{2}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

2.

For any
(s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\|F^{i}(0,s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})\|_{2}^{2}=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{(s,a^{i})}\left([F^{i}(0,s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})](s,a^{i})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\left(R_{i}(s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})+\gamma v^{i}(s_{1})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where the last line follows from
\|v^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
and
|R_{i}(s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})|\leq 1
.

3.

We first write the operator
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(\cdot)
explicitly. Using the definition of
\mathcal{T}^{i}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q^{i})(s)=\mu_{k}(s)\text{diag}(\pi_{k}^{i}(s))\left(\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)-q^{i}(s)\right),\quad\forall\,s\in\mathcal{S}.

Since
\mu_{k}(s)\geq\mu_{\min}>0
by Lemma
4.7
(4), and
\text{diag}(\pi_{k}^{i}(s))
has strictly positive diagonal entries by Lemma
4.2
, the equation
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q^{i})=0
has a unique solution
\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}
, given by

\displaystyle\bar{q}_{k}^{i}(s)=\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s),\quad\forall\,s\in\mathcal{S}.

4.

Using the expression of
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(\cdot)
, we have for any
q_{1}^{i},q_{2}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}
that

\displaystyle(q_{1}^{i}-q_{2}^{i})^{\top}(\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{1}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{2}^{i}))=\,

\displaystyle-\sum_{s,a^{i}}\mu_{k}(s)\pi_{k}^{i}(a^{i}|s)(q_{1}^{i}(s,a^{i})-q_{2}^{i}(s,a^{i}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-\min_{s,a^{i}}\mu_{k}(s)\pi_{k}^{i}(a^{i}|s)\|q_{1}^{i}-q_{2}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-\mu_{\min}\ell_{\tau}\|q_{1}^{i}-q_{2}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}

(Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
4.7
)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-c_{\tau}\|q_{1}^{i}-q_{2}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

C.5.4  Proof of Lemma
4.7

Let
\Pi_{\det}
denote the set of deterministic stationary policy pairs. Since the state and action spaces are finite,
\Pi_{\det}
is finite and
\Pi
is compact.

For each
\pi\in\Pi_{\det}
, Assumption
3.2
implies that the transition matrix
P_{\pi}
of the induced Markov chain
\{S_{k}\}
is irreducible and aperiodic. Hence, there exists a positive integer
r(\pi)
such that
P_{\pi}^{r}
has strictly positive entries for all
r\geq r(\pi)
; see, e.g.,
[
38
, Proposition 1.7]
. Define
r_{*}:=\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\det}}r(\pi)
.
Then
P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})>0
for all
\pi\in\Pi_{\det}
and all
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.

We next show that the same positivity holds uniformly over all stationary policy pairs. Fix any
\pi\in\Pi
. For each state
s\in\mathcal{S}
and each player
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
a^{i}(s)\in\mathcal{A}^{i}
be an action such that
\pi^{i}(a^{i}(s)\mid s)>0
.
Let
d=(d^{1},d^{2})
be the deterministic stationary policy pair defined by
d^{i}(s)=a^{i}(s)
. Since
d\in\Pi_{\det}
, we have
P_{d}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})>0
for all
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
. Therefore, for any
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
, there exists at least one path
s=s_{0},s_{1},\ldots,s_{r_{*}}=s^{\prime}

such that

\displaystyle\prod_{\ell=0}^{r_{*}-1}P(s_{\ell+1}\mid s_{\ell},d^{1}(s_{\ell}),d^{2}(s_{\ell}))>0.

Along the same path, under the stationary policy pair
\pi
, the probability of choosing the action pair

(d^{1}(s_{\ell}),d^{2}(s_{\ell}))
at state
s_{\ell}
is positive for every
\ell
. Hence
P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})>0
for all
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Since
P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})
is continuous in
\pi
and
\Pi
is compact, we have

\displaystyle p_{*}:=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})>0.

(C.14)

The constants
r_{*}
and
p_{*}
depend only on the transition kernel and the finite state-action spaces, and not on
\ell_{\tau}
.

We now prove the four claims.

1.

Since (
C.14
) implies that, for every
\pi\in\Pi
, the transition matrix
P_{\pi}
of the induced Markov chain
\{S_{k}\}
has a strictly positive power,
P_{\pi}
is irreducible and aperiodic
[
38
, Proposition 1.7]
. Therefore, there exists a unique stationary distribution
\mu_{\pi}
for each
\pi\in\Pi
.

2.

Fix
\pi\in\Pi
. By (
C.14
) and the fact that
\mu_{\pi}(s^{\prime})\leq 1
for every
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
, we have the minorization condition

\displaystyle P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})\geq p_{*}\mu_{\pi}(s^{\prime}),\quad\forall s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}.

(C.15)

Equivalently, for every
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}=p_{*}\mathbf{1}\mu_{\pi}^{\top}+(1-p_{*})Q_{\pi}
,
where
\mathbf{1}
is the all-ones vector and
Q_{\pi}
is a transition kernel. Now let
\xi
be such that
\xi^{\top}\mathbf{1}=0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\xi^{\top}P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}\|_{1}=\|p_{*}\xi^{\top}\mathbf{1}\mu_{\pi}^{\top}+(1-p_{*})\xi^{\top}Q_{\pi}\|_{1}=(1-p_{*})\|\xi^{\top}Q_{\pi}\|_{1}\leq(1-p_{*})\|\xi\|_{1}.

(C.16)

Let
k=qr_{*}+r
with
q=\lfloor k/r_{*}\rfloor
and
0\leq r<r_{*}
. For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
, let
\xi_{s}:=\delta_{s}-\mu_{\pi}
, where
\delta_{s}
denotes the binary vector with its
s
-th entry being one and zero everywhere else.
Since
\mu_{\pi}^{\top}P_{\pi}=\mu_{\pi}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{\pi}^{k}(s,\cdot)-\mu_{\pi}(\cdot)\|_{\text{TV}}=\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\xi_{s}^{\top}(P_{\pi}^{r_{*}})^{q}P_{\pi}^{r}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1-p_{*})^{q}\|\xi_{s}^{\top}P_{\pi}^{r}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1-p_{*})^{q}\|\xi_{s}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-p_{*})^{q}.

Define
\rho_{*}:=\exp(-p_{*}/(2r_{*}))\in(0,1).

Using
(1-p_{*})^{q}\leq 2\rho_{*}^{k}
for all
k\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|P_{\pi}^{k}(s,\cdot)-\mu_{\pi}(\cdot)\|_{\text{TV}}\leq 2\rho_{*}^{k},\quad\forall\,k\geq 0.

Consequently,
\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|P_{\pi}^{k}(s,\cdot)-\mu_{\pi}(\cdot)\|_{\text{TV}}\leq\eta
holds whenever
2\rho_{*}^{k}\leq\eta
, that is,

\displaystyle k\geq\frac{\log(2/\eta)}{\log(1/\rho_{*})}.

3.

Fix

\pi=(\pi^{1},\pi^{2}),\bar{\pi}=(\bar{\pi}^{1},\bar{\pi}^{2})\in\Pi
and write

P=P_{\pi}
,
\bar{P}=P_{\bar{\pi}}
,
\mu=\mu_{\pi}
, and
\bar{\mu}=\mu_{\bar{\pi}}
. Define

\displaystyle\Delta(\pi,\bar{\pi}):=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|\pi^{1}(s)-\bar{\pi}^{1}(s)\|_{1}+\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|\pi^{2}(s)-\bar{\pi}^{2}(s)\|_{1}.

For each
s\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\|P(s,\cdot)-\bar{P}(s,\cdot)\|_{1}\leq\|\pi^{1}(s)-\bar{\pi}^{1}(s)\|_{1}+\|\pi^{2}(s)-\bar{\pi}^{2}(s)\|_{1}\leq\Delta(\pi,\bar{\pi}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|P(s,\cdot)-\bar{P}(s,\cdot)\|_{1}\leq\Delta(\pi,\bar{\pi}).

(C.17)

Let
x:=(P-\bar{P})^{\top}\mu
. Since both
P
and
\bar{P}
are transition matrices,
x^{\top}\mathbf{1}=0
. Moreover, by (
C.17
),

\displaystyle\|x\|_{1}=\|\mu^{\top}(P-\bar{P})\|_{1}\leq\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|P(s,\cdot)-\bar{P}(s,\cdot)\|_{1}\leq\Delta(\pi,\bar{\pi}).

(C.18)

Since
\bar{P}
is irreducible and aperiodic by Part (1) of this lemma, we have
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mu^{\top}\bar{P}^{N}\to\bar{\mu}^{\top}

[
38
, Theorem 4.9]
. Hence

\displaystyle\mu-\bar{\mu}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu(I-\bar{P}^{N})=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mu(I-\bar{P})\bar{P}^{j}.

Since
\mu^{\top}P=\mu^{\top}
, we have
\mu^{\top}(I-\bar{P})=\mu^{\top}(P-\bar{P})=x
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mu-\bar{\mu}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|x\bar{P}^{j}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1-p_{*})^{\lfloor j/r_{*}\rfloor}\right)\Delta(\pi,\bar{\pi})

(Inequalities (
C.16
) and (
C.18
))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r_{*}}{p_{*}}\Delta(\pi,\bar{\pi}).

Thus Part (3) holds with
L_{p}=r_{*}/p_{*}
,
which is independent of
\ell_{\tau}
.

4.

Finally, for any
\pi\in\Pi
, using stationarity and (
C.14
), we have for every
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\pi}(s^{\prime})=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mu_{\pi}(s)P_{\pi}^{r_{*}}(s,s^{\prime})\geq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mu_{\pi}(s)p_{*}=p_{*}.

Taking the infimum over
\pi\in\Pi
and the minimum over
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
gives

\displaystyle\mu_{\min}=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\mu_{\pi}(s^{\prime})\geq p_{*}>0.

C.5.5  Proof of Lemma
4.9

Using
\|\cdot\|_{2}^{2}
as a Lyapunov function, we have by the update equation (
4.6
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}+q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}+\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})^{\top}\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}[(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})^{\top}(q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}[(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})^{\top}(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-2\alpha_{k}c_{\tau})\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}[(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})^{\top}(q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}[(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})^{\top}(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})],

(C.19)

where the last line follows from Lemma
4.8
(4).

The terms
\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})^{\top}(q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i})]
, and
\mathbb{E}[(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})^{\top}(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})]
on the right-hand side of (
C.19
) are bounded in the following lemma, whose proof is presented in Appendix
C.7.1
.

Lemma C.3
.

The following inequalities hold for all
k\geq 0
.

1.

\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\frac{4nm\alpha_{k}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

2.

\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\frac{4nm\beta_{k}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

3.

\mathbb{E}[\langle q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle]\leq\frac{4nm\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

4.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle]\leq\frac{17nm^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{16}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)].

We next consider the last term on the right-hand side of (
C.19
), which involves the difference between the operator
F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})
and its expected version
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})
, and hence can be viewed as the stochastic error due to sampling. Since the sample at time
k
is generated using
\pi_{k+1}
while
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}
is defined using the stationary distribution and action distribution associated with
\pi_{k}
, the term
F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})
contains both the Markovian sampling error and the one-step policy-shift error. Lemma
C.4
controls both effects using the slow variation of the policies and the Lipschitz sensitivity of the stationary distribution.

The fact that the Markov chain
\{(S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\}
is time-inhomogeneous presents a challenge in our analysis. To overcome this challenge, observe that: (1) the policy, and hence the transition probability matrix of the induced Markov chain, is changing slowly compared to the
q
-function; see Algorithm
5
, Line 3, and (2) the stationary distribution as a function of the policy is Lipschitz; see Lemma
4.7
(3). These two observations together enable us to develop a refined conditioning argument to handle the time-inhomogeneous Markovian noise. The result is presented in the following lemma, whose proof is presented in Appendix
C.7.2
.

Lemma C.4
.

The following inequality holds for all
k\geq z_{k}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]\leq\frac{17z_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where we recall that
\alpha_{k_{1},k_{2}}=\sum_{k=k_{1}}^{k_{2}}\alpha_{k}
.

Using the upper bounds we obtained for all the terms on the right-hand side of (
C.19
), we have the one-step Lyapunov drift inequality for
q_{k}^{i}
. Specifically, for
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have from (
C.19
), Lemma
C.3
, and Lemma
C.4
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-2\alpha_{k}c_{\tau})\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{4nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}(\alpha_{k}^{2}+2\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}+\beta_{k}^{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{34nm^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{8}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)]+\frac{34z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-2\alpha_{k}c_{\tau}+\frac{34nm^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{8}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)]

\displaystyle+\frac{50nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1},

where the second inequality follows from
\beta_{k}=c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha_{k}
with
c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq 1
, the monotonicity of
\{\alpha_{k}\}
, and
z_{k}\geq 1
, which imply
\alpha_{k}^{2}+2\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}+\beta_{k}^{2}\leq 4\alpha_{k}^{2}\leq 4z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}
.
Since

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq\frac{c_{\tau}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}{34nm^{2}},

(Condition
3.1
)

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha_{k}c_{\tau}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{8}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)]+\frac{50nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}.

Summing the previous inequality over
i=1,2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha_{k}c_{\tau}\right)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)]+\frac{100nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}.

This completes the proof.
∎

C.6  Solving Coupled Lyapunov Drift Inequalities

We first restate the Lyapunov drift inequalities from previous sections. Recall our notation

\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)=\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{t,k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{t,k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v_{t},s}(\pi_{t,k}^{1}(s),\pi_{t,k}^{2}(s))
,

\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)=\|v_{t}^{1}+v_{t}^{2}\|_{\infty}
, and

\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)=\sum_{i=1,2}\|v_{t}^{i}-v_{*}^{i}\|_{\infty}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{t}
be the history of Algorithm
3
right before the
t
-th outer-loop iteration. Note that
v_{t}^{1}
and
v_{t}^{2}
are both measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{t}
. In what follows, for ease of presentation, we write
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\cdot]
for
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]
.

•

Lemma
4.3
:
It holds for all
t\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)+4\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)+6\tau\log(m).

(C.20)

•

Lemma
4.4
:
It holds for all
t\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t+1)\leq\gamma\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K).

(C.21)

•

Lemma
4.6
:
It holds for all
t,k\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]+\frac{16m^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{32m^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau^{3}\ell_{\tau}^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]+2L_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}.

(C.22)

•

Lemma
4.9
:
It holds for all
t\geq 0
and
k\geq z_{k}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha_{k}c_{\tau}\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{4}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]

(C.23)

\displaystyle+\frac{100nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}.

Adding (
C.22
) and (
C.23
), and using

c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq\min\{L_{\tau}^{-1/2},c_{\tau}\tau^{3}\ell_{\tau}^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}/(128m^{2}),c_{\tau}\}
from Condition
3.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k+1)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]

\displaystyle+\frac{16m^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}+\frac{102nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}.

(C.24)

C.6.1  Constant Stepsize

When using constant stepsizes, i.e.,
\alpha_{k}\equiv\alpha
and
\beta_{k}\equiv\beta=c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha
, iterating (
C.24
) from
z_{\beta}
to
k
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{k-z_{\beta}}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,0)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,0))

\displaystyle+\frac{32m^{2}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}+\frac{204nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha.

(C.25)

We next bound
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,0)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,0)
. For
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,0)=\,

\displaystyle\max_{s}V_{v_{t},s}(\pi_{t,0}^{1}(s),\pi_{t,0}^{2}(s))

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\max_{s}\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{\mu^{i}}\left\{(\mu^{i}-\pi_{t,0}^{i}(s))^{\top}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s)\pi_{t,0}^{-i}(s)+\tau\nu(\mu^{i})-\tau\nu(\pi_{t,0}^{i}(s))\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i=1,2}\max_{s,a^{i},a^{-i}}|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v_{t}^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})|+2\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\gamma}+2\tau\log(m),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,0)=\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{t,0}^{i}-\bar{q}_{t,0}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{8nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(Lemma
4.2
)

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,0)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,0)\leq\frac{4}{1-\gamma}+2\tau\log(m)+\frac{8nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}=L_{\text{in}}.

Using this bound in (
C.25
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{k-z_{\beta}}+\frac{32m^{2}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}+\frac{204nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha.

(C.26)

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{k-z_{\beta}}+\frac{32m^{2}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}+\frac{204nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha.

Substituting this inequality into (
C.23
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha c_{\tau}\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]+\frac{151nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta L_{\text{in}}}{4}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{k-z_{\beta}}+\frac{8m^{2}\beta}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}.

Iterating the previous inequality and using
c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq c_{\tau}
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\left(1-c_{\tau}\alpha\right)^{k-z_{\beta}}+\frac{\beta L_{\text{in}}(k-z_{\beta})}{4}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{k-z_{\beta}-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{8m^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}{c_{\tau}\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}+\frac{151nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\tau}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha.

Thus, by Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)^{1/2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}\left(1-c_{\tau}\alpha\right)^{\frac{k-z_{\beta}}{2}}+\frac{\beta^{1/2}L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}(k-z_{\beta})^{1/2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{k-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3mc_{\alpha,\beta}^{1/2}}{c_{\tau}^{1/2}\tau^{1/2}}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+\frac{13n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}.

Substituting the previous bound into (
C.21
) and then taking total expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+2L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}\left(1-c_{\tau}\alpha\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}}{2}}

\displaystyle+\beta^{1/2}L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{6mc_{\alpha,\beta}^{1/2}}{c_{\tau}^{1/2}\tau^{1/2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+\frac{26n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+2L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}\left(1-c_{\tau}\alpha\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}}{2}}

\displaystyle+\beta^{1/2}L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}+\frac{26n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2},

where the last line follows from

c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq c_{\tau}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}/(144m^{2})
; see Condition
3.1
. Since
\|v_{0}^{1}+v_{0}^{2}\|_{\infty}\leq 2/(1-\gamma)
, iterating the previous inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+\frac{4L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}\left(1-c_{\tau}\alpha\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}}{2}}}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\beta^{1/2}L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}}{1-\gamma}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}+\frac{52n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\tau}^{1/2}}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+\frac{6L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}}{1-\gamma}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}+\frac{52n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\tau}^{1/2}}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}.

(C.27)

Now we have obtained finite-sample bounds for
\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)
,
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)
. The next step is to use them in (
C.20
) to obtain a finite-sample bound for
\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)
. Specifically, using (
C.20
), (
C.26
), and (
C.27
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+4\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+2\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)]+4\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)]+6\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+\frac{266m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{805m^{2}L_{\text{in}}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\tau}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1223nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha^{1/2}+6\tau\log(m).

Iterating the previous inequality from
0
to
T-1
and using
\mathcal{L}_{v}(0)\leq 4/(1-\gamma)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{270m^{2}T}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{T-1}+\frac{805m^{2}L_{\text{in}}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{3}}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1223nm}{(1-\gamma)^{3}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha^{1/2}+\frac{6\tau\log(m)}{1-\gamma}.

Our next step is to use the bounds obtained for
\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)
,
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)
,
\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)
in Lemma
4.1
. For simplicity of presentation, we use
a\lesssim b
to mean that there exists a numerical constant
c
such that
a\leq cb
. Using the previous inequality, (
C.26
), and (
C.27
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{T,K}^{1},\pi_{T,K}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{8}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)]+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)]+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)]+\frac{8\tau\log(m)}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}T}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{3}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{T-1}+\frac{m^{2}L_{\text{in}}(K-z_{\beta})^{1/2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{\frac{K-z_{\beta}-1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{nm}{(1-\gamma)^{4}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha^{1/2}+\frac{\tau\log(m)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The proof of Theorem
3.4
(1) is complete.

C.6.2  Diminishing Stepsizes

Consider using harmonically diminishing stepsizes, i.e.,
\alpha_{k}=\frac{\alpha}{k+h}
,
\beta_{k}=\frac{\beta}{k+h}
, and
\beta=c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha
. Iterating (
C.24
), we have for all
k\geq k_{0}:=\min\{k^{\prime}\mid k^{\prime}\geq z_{k^{\prime}}\}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\underbrace{\prod_{m=k_{0}}^{k-1}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{m}}{2}\right)}_{\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{1}}+\frac{204nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\underbrace{\sum_{n=k_{0}}^{k-1}z_{n}^{2}\alpha_{n}^{2}\prod_{m=n+1}^{k-1}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{m}}{2}\right)}_{\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{16m^{2}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}\underbrace{\sum_{n=k_{0}}^{k-1}\beta_{n}\prod_{m=n+1}^{k-1}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{m}}{2}\right)}_{\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{3}}.

Next, we evaluate the terms
\{\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq 3}
. Terms of this form have been well studied in the existing literature
[
51
,
35
,
20
]
. Specifically, using the same line of analysis as in
[
20
, Appendix A.2]
and
\beta=4
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\leq\frac{k_{0}+h}{k+h},\quad\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\leq\frac{64ez_{k}^{2}}{(k+h)c_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}},\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{\mathcal{E}}_{3}\leq 2.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)+\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\frac{k_{0}+h}{k+h}+\frac{3264enm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{k}^{2}\alpha_{k}+\frac{32m^{2}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\frac{k_{0}+h}{k+h}+\frac{3264enm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{k}^{2}\alpha_{k}+\frac{32m^{2}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}.

(C.28)

Using the previous inequality on
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]
in (
C.23
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha_{k}c_{\tau}\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]+\frac{100nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{\text{in}}c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha_{k}}{4}\frac{k_{0}+h}{k+h}+\frac{816enm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}z_{k}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+\frac{8m^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha_{k}c_{\tau}\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]+\frac{1017eL_{\text{in}}nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\alpha_{k_{0}}}z_{k}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+\frac{8m^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}.

Here the last inequality uses the monotonicity of
\{\alpha_{k}\}
, the definition of
k_{0}
, and the fact that
z_{k}=\mathcal{O}(\log k)
; the constants are numerical and chosen conservatively.

Iterating the previous inequality starting from
k_{0}
, and using
\alpha c_{\tau}\geq 1
from Condition
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle L_{\text{in}}\frac{k_{0}+h}{k+h}+\frac{4068e^{2}L_{\text{in}}nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\tau}\alpha_{k_{0}}}z_{k}^{2}\alpha_{k}+\frac{8m^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}{c_{\tau}\tau}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)^{2}.

Therefore, by Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)^{1/2}]\leq L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}\left(\frac{k_{0}+h}{k+h}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{64eL_{\text{in}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}}z_{k}\alpha_{k}^{1/2}+\frac{3mc_{\alpha,\beta}^{1/2}}{c_{\tau}^{1/2}\tau^{1/2}}\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t).

(C.29)

Taking total expectation on both sides of the previous inequality and then using the result in (
C.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+2L_{\text{in}}^{1/2}\left(\frac{k_{0}+h}{K+h}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{128eL_{\text{in}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}}z_{K}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}+\frac{6mc_{\alpha,\beta}^{1/2}}{c_{\tau}^{1/2}\tau^{1/2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\gamma+1}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+\frac{130eL_{\text{in}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}}z_{K}\alpha_{K}^{1/2},

where the last line follows from
c_{\alpha,\beta}\leq\frac{c_{\tau}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}{144m^{2}}
; see Condition
3.1
. Iterating the previous inequality starting from
0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+\frac{260eL_{\text{in}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\tau}^{1/2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}}z_{K}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}.

(C.30)

The next step is to bound
\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)
. Recall from (
C.20
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)]+4\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)]+6\tau\log(m).

Using (
C.28
), (
C.29
), and (
C.30
) in the previous inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+4\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+2\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)]+4\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)]+6\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+\frac{130eL_{\text{in}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}m^{1/2}}{(1-\gamma)c_{\tau}^{1/2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}}z_{K}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{4L_{\text{in}}\alpha_{K}}{\alpha_{k_{0}}}+\frac{13056enm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{K}^{2}\alpha_{K}+6\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle+\frac{522m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+\frac{67860eL_{\text{in}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}m^{5/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\tau c_{\tau}^{1/2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}}z_{K}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+\frac{522m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+\frac{15056eL_{\text{in}}nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{K}^{2}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}+6\tau\log(m).

Iterating the previous inequality from
0
to
T-1
and using
\mathcal{L}_{v}(0)\leq\frac{4}{1-\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)]\leq\frac{526m^{2}T}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{T-1}+\frac{15056eL_{\text{in}}nm}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{K}^{2}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}+\frac{6\tau\log(m)}{1-\gamma}.

Finally, using the previous inequality, (
C.28
), and (
C.30
) in Lemma
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{T,K}^{1},\pi_{T,K}^{2})]\lesssim\frac{m^{2}T}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{3}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\right)^{T-1}+\frac{L_{\text{in}}nm}{(1-\gamma)^{4}\alpha_{k_{0}}^{1/2}c_{\alpha,\beta}}z_{K}^{2}\alpha_{K}^{1/2}+\frac{\tau\log(m)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The proof of Theorem
3.4
(2) is complete.

C.7  Proofs of Supporting Lemmas

C.7.1  Proof of Lemma
C.3

1.

For any
k\geq 0
, using the update equation (
4.6
) and Lemma
4.8
(1), we have

\displaystyle\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}=\,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{2}\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{2}\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-F^{i}(0,S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})

\displaystyle+F^{i}(0,S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{nm}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}

(
\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
by Lemma
4.2
)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4nm\alpha_{k}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The result follows by taking expectation on both sides of the previous inequality.

2.

For any
k\geq 0
, using the definition of
\bar{q}_{k}^{i}
in Appendix
C.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\pi_{k+1}^{-i}(s)-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\beta_{k}^{2}\sum_{s}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\beta_{k}^{2}\sum_{s}\left(\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4nm\beta_{k}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The result follows by taking expectation on both sides of the previous inequality.

3.

For any
k\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\langle q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle\leq\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{2}\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}\leq\frac{4nm\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where the last inequality follows from Parts (1) and (2). The result follows by taking expectation on both sides.

4.

For any
k\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\langle q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\beta_{k}\sum_{s}\langle q_{k}^{i}(s)-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}(s),\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\beta_{k}}{2}\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\beta_{k}}{2c_{1}}\sum_{s}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}^{2},

(C.31)

where
c_{1}>0
is arbitrary. We next bound the second term on the right-hand side. For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}(s))+\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v^{-i})(s)\pi_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\underbrace{\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}(s)))\|_{2}}_{B_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v^{-i})(s)\pi_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}}_{B_{2}}.

Since the softmax operator
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
is
\frac{1}{\tau}
– Lipschitz continuous with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}

[
27
, Proposition 4]
, we have

\displaystyle B_{1}\leq\,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{m}{\tau(1-\gamma)}\|q_{k}^{-i}(s)-\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}.

We next analyze
B_{2}
. Using Lemma
4.5
(1) and the quadratic growth property of strongly convex functions, we have

\displaystyle B_{2}=\,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v^{-i})(s)\pi_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}\|\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{-i}(v^{-i})(s)\pi_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}m}{\sqrt{\tau}(1-\gamma)}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))^{1/2}.

Combining the upper bounds for
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{-i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{-i}(s))\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{s}(B_{1}+B_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s}(B_{1}^{2}+B_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\tau^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\|q_{k}^{-i}(s)-\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2m^{2}}{\tau^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\|q_{k}^{-i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{4nm^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k).

Returning to (
C.31
), we have

\displaystyle\langle q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\beta_{k}}{2}\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{m^{2}\beta_{k}}{c_{1}\tau^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\|q_{k}^{-i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2nm^{2}\beta_{k}}{c_{1}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k).

Choosing
c_{1}=\frac{32nm^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}
and taking expectations on both sides gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{16nm^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{32n\tau}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{-i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{-i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{16}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)].

In particular, after summing over
i=1,2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\langle q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i},\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\rangle]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{17nm^{2}\beta_{k}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{\beta_{k}}{8}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)].

This completes the proof.
∎

C.7.2  Proof of Lemma
C.4

For any
k\geq z_{k}
, we decompose

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k-z_{k}}^{i})]}_{N_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})]}_{N_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}))^{\top}(\bar{q}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]}_{N_{3}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]}_{N_{4}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[(\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]}_{N_{5}}.

(C.32)

To bound
N_{1}
to
N_{5}
, we first use the following lemma.

Lemma C.5
.

For any positive integers
k_{1}\leq k_{2}
, we have

\|q_{k_{2}}^{i}-q_{k_{1}}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\alpha_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}}{1-\gamma}
and

\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|\pi_{k_{2}}^{i}(s)-\pi_{k_{1}}^{i}(s)\|_{1}\leq 2\beta_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.
For any
k\in[k_{1},k_{2}-1]
, we have by (
4.6
) that

\displaystyle\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}=\alpha_{k}\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{1-\gamma}.

It follows that
\|q_{k_{2}}^{i}-q_{k_{1}}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\alpha_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}}{1-\gamma}
. Similarly, for any
k\in[k_{1},k_{2}-1]
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle\|\pi_{k+1}^{i}(s)-\pi_{k}^{i}(s)\|_{1}=\beta_{k}\|\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{i}(s)\|_{1}\leq 2\beta_{k}.

Thus,
\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|\pi_{k_{2}}^{i}(s)-\pi_{k_{1}}^{i}(s)\|_{1}\leq 2\beta_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}
.
∎∎

Let
\mathcal{F}_{k}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by

\{S_{0},A_{0}^{i},A_{0}^{-i},\cdots,S_{k-1},A_{k-1}^{i},A_{k-1}^{-i},S_{k}\}
.

The Term
N_{1}
.

Using the tower property of conditional expectations, we have

\displaystyle N_{1}=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\mathbb{E}[F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-z_{k}}]-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\big)^{\top}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\big]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathbb{E}[F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-z_{k}}]-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathbb{E}[F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-z_{k}}]-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\big].

(C.33)

The first term on the right-hand side of (
C.33
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\leq\frac{8nmL_{p}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{1-\gamma}.

(C.34)

Indeed, this follows from the total-variation characterization, Lemma
4.7
(3), and Lemma
C.5
.

It remains to bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
C.33
). Since the sample at time
k
is generated using
\pi_{k+1}
, while
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}
is defined using the stationary distribution and action distribution associated with
\pi_{k}
, this term contains both a state-distribution mixing error and a one-step policy-shift error. Using the definition of conditional expectations, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-z_{k}}]-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left\{\left\|\prod_{j=k+1}^{k+z_{k}}P_{\pi_{j-z_{k}}}-P_{\pi_{k}}^{z_{k}}\right\|_{\infty}+2\rho_{*}^{z_{k}}\right\}+\frac{4\beta_{k}}{1-\gamma}.

(C.35)

The last term
\frac{4\beta_{k}}{1-\gamma}
accounts for the one-step difference between sampling actions from
\pi_{k+1}
and using the action distribution
\pi_{k}
in
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{j=k+1}^{k+z_{k}}P_{\pi_{j-z_{k}}}-P_{\pi_{k}}^{z_{k}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{z_{k}}\|P_{\pi_{k-\ell+1}}-P_{\pi_{k}}\|_{\infty}\leq 4z_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.5
. Using the definition of
z_{k}
, we have
2\rho_{*}^{z_{k}}\leq\beta_{k}
. Hence, using
z_{k}\geq 1
and
\beta_{k}\leq\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-z_{k}}]-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\left(4z_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}+\beta_{k}\right)+\frac{4\beta_{k}}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{14z_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{1-\gamma}.

Using the previous inequality and (
C.34
) in (
C.33
), we obtain

\displaystyle N_{1}\leq\frac{16L_{p}nm\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{28z_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\leq\frac{44L_{p}nmz_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The Term
N_{2}
.

For any
k\geq z_{k}
, by Lemma
C.5
,

\displaystyle N_{2}\leq\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\|q_{k}^{i}-q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}\|_{\infty}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{1}+\|\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}].

(C.36)

Using the definition of
F^{i}(\cdot)
and Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{1}\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}.

(C.37)

Moreover, by Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}.

(C.38)

Using (
C.37
) and (
C.38
) in (
C.36
), we have

\displaystyle N_{2}\leq\frac{8\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The Term
N_{3}
.

For any
k\geq z_{k}
,

\displaystyle N_{3}\leq\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\|\bar{q}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}],

where the last line follows from (
C.37
) and (
C.38
). Observe that

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}=\,

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)(\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)-\pi_{k-z_{k}}^{-i}(s))\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{1,\infty}\|\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)-\pi_{k-z_{k}}^{-i}(s)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{1-\gamma},

where the last line follows from Lemma
C.5
and

\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{1,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle N_{3}\leq\frac{8\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

The Term
N_{4}
.

For any
k\geq z_{k}
,

\displaystyle N_{4}\leq\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{1}\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{\infty}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-F^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.5
.

The Term
N_{5}
.

For any
k\geq z_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle N_{5}\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})\|_{1}+\|\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{16L_{p}nm\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}],

(C.39)

where the last line follows from the same analysis used to obtain (
C.34
). For the second term on the right-hand side of (
C.39
), using the definition of
\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})-\bar{F}_{k-z_{k}}^{i}(q_{k-z_{k}}^{i})\|_{1}\leq\|q_{k}^{i}-q_{k-z_{k}}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{1-\gamma}.

Using this in (
C.39
), we obtain

\displaystyle N_{5}\leq\frac{16L_{p}nm\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{4\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

Combining the upper bounds for
N_{1}
to
N_{5}
in (
C.32
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{44L_{p}nmz_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{8\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{8\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{4\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{16L_{p}nm\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{4\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{76L_{p}nmz_{k}\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{16\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

Since
c_{\alpha,\beta}=\beta_{k}/\alpha_{k}\leq 1/(76L_{p}nm)
(cf. Condition
3.1
), we have
\beta_{k-z_{k},k-1}\leq c_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}
, which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]\leq\frac{17z_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
3.5

The high-level idea of proving Theorem
3.5
is similar to that of Theorem
3.4
. However, due to the algorithmic modification, namely, replacing
\sigma_{\tau}(\cdot)
with
\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(\cdot)
, the negative drift inequalities for the policies and the
q
-functions are substantially different from those in the proof of Theorem
3.4
.

D.1  Boundedness of the Iterates

Lemma D.1
.

For all
t,k
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have
\|v_{t}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
,
\|q_{t,k}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
, and
\min_{s\in\mathcal{S},a^{i}\in\mathcal{A}^{i}}\pi_{t,k}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\bar{\epsilon}/m
.

The proof of Lemma
D.1
is identical to that of Lemma
4.2
, and therefore is omitted.

D.2  Bounding the Nash Gap

We restate Lemma
4.1
to make the proof self-contained.

Lemma D.2
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\text{NG}(\pi_{T,K}^{1},\pi_{T,K}^{2})\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)+2\tau\log(m)\right).

D.3  Analysis of the Outer Loop

We restate Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.4
to make the proof self-contained.

Lemma D.3
.

The following inequality holds for all
t\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)+4\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)+6\tau\log(m).

Lemma D.4
.

The following inequality holds for all
t\geq 0
:

\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t+1)\leq\gamma\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)
.

D.4  Analysis of the Inner Loop

For ease of presentation, we write down only the inner loop of Algorithm
4
in the following, where we omit the subscript
t
. Similarly, we write
\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)
for
\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)
and
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)
for
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)
.

Algorithm 6

Inner Loop of Algorithm
4

1:

Input:
Integer
K
, initializations
q_{0}^{i}
and
\pi_{0}^{i}
, and a joint
v
-function
v^{i}
from the outer loop. Note that we have
\|q_{0}^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
,
\|v^{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
, and
\min_{s,a^{i}}\pi_{0}^{i}(a^{i}\mid s)\geq\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{m}
due to Lemma
D.1
.

2:

for

k=0,1,\cdots,K-1

do

3:

\pi_{k+1}^{i}(s)=\pi_{k}^{i}(s)+\beta_{k}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{i}(s))
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}

4:

Sample

A_{k}^{i}\sim\pi_{k+1}^{i}(\cdot\mid S_{k})
, and observe
S_{k+1}\sim p(\cdot\mid S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})

5:

q_{k+1}^{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})=q_{k}^{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})+\alpha_{k}\left(\mathcal{R}_{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i})+\gamma v^{i}(S_{k+1})-q_{k}^{i}(S_{k},A_{k}^{i})\right)

6:

end

for

D.4.1  Analysis of the Policies

Lemma D.5
.

When choosing
\bar{\epsilon}=\tau\leq 1
, we have for all
k\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(k)]+4\beta_{k}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}+\frac{8m\beta_{k}}{(1-\gamma)\tau}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)^{1/2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{12m\tau\beta_{k}}{1-\gamma}+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

where
\bar{L}_{\tau}=\frac{5m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
D.5
.
We will use
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
as a Lyapunov function to study the evolution of
(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))
. A sequence of properties regarding
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
for arbitrary matrices
X_{1},X_{2}
is presented in Lemma
D.8
. To begin with, we identify the smoothness parameter of
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Using Lemma
D.8
(1) and the definition of
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\frac{\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2}^{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}+\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 2\left(m+\frac{\max(\|\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)\|_{2}^{2},\|\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}+\|\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)+\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)^{\top}\|_{2}\right)

(
\bar{\epsilon}=\tau
)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left(m+\frac{m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{2m}{1-\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{5m^{2}}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}=\bar{L}_{\tau},

where the first inequality follows from
|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})|\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for all
(s,a^{i},a^{-i})
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, and the last inequality follows from
m\geq 2
and
\tau\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
. Therefore,
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
is a
\bar{L}_{\tau}
– smooth function on
\Pi_{\bar{\epsilon}}
, where
\Pi_{\bar{\epsilon}}=\{(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\mid\min_{a^{1}}\mu^{1}(a^{1})\geq\bar{\epsilon}/m,\min_{a^{2}}\mu^{2}(a^{2})\geq\bar{\epsilon}/m\}
.

Using the smoothness of
V_{v,s}(\cdot,\cdot)
, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
, we have by the policy update equation in Algorithm
6
, Line 3, that

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k+1}^{1}(s),\pi_{k+1}^{2}(s))

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{2}(s))-\pi_{k}^{2}(s)\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{1}(s))-\pi_{k}^{1}(s)\rangle+\frac{\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}}{2}\sum_{i=1,2}\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{i}(s))-\pi_{k}^{i}(s)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)\pi_{k}^{1}(s))-\pi_{k}^{2}(s)\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)\pi_{k}^{2}(s))-\pi_{k}^{1}(s)\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{2}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{2}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{2}(v^{2})(s)\pi_{k}^{1}(s))\rangle

\displaystyle+\beta_{k}\langle\nabla_{1}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s)),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q_{k}^{1}(s))-\sigma_{\tau}(\mathcal{T}^{1}(v^{1})(s)\pi_{k}^{2}(s))\rangle+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+4\beta_{k}\max_{s,a^{1},a^{2}}\left|\sum_{i=1,2}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})\right|

\displaystyle+2\beta_{k}\left(m+\frac{\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}}{\tau}\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}(s)-\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\pi_{k}^{-i}(s)\|_{2}

\displaystyle+4\beta_{k}\bar{\epsilon}\left(m+\sum_{i=1,2}\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}\right)+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2},

where the last line follows from Lemma
D.8
(2) and (3), and
\bar{\epsilon}=\tau
.

Since

\displaystyle\max_{s,a^{i},a^{-i}}\left|\sum_{i=1,2}\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s,a^{i},a^{-i})\right|\leq\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty},\quad\|\mathcal{T}^{i}(v^{i})(s)\|_{2}\leq\frac{m}{1-\gamma},

and
\tau\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k+1}^{1}(s),\pi_{k+1}^{2}(s))\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta_{k})\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}V_{v,s}(\pi_{k}^{1}(s),\pi_{k}^{2}(s))+4\beta_{k}\|v^{1}+v^{2}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\frac{4m\beta_{k}}{\tau(1-\gamma)}\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta_{k}^{2}+\frac{12m\tau\beta_{k}}{1-\gamma}.

Taking expectation on both sides and using the pathwise inequality

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}\leq 2\left(\sum_{i=1,2}\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}=2\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)^{1/2},

gives the desired result.
∎∎

D.4.2  Analysis of the
q
-Functions

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, fixing
v^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let
F^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}^{i}\times\mathcal{A}^{-i}\times\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}
be defined as

\displaystyle[F^{i}(q^{i},s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})](s,a^{i})=\mathds{1}_{\{(s,a^{i})=(s_{0},a_{0}^{i})\}}\left(R_{i}(s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i})+\gamma v^{i}(s_{1})-q^{i}(s_{0},a_{0}^{i})\right)

for all
(q^{i},s_{0},a_{0}^{i},a_{0}^{-i},s_{1})
and
(s,a^{i})
. Then, Line 5 of Algorithm
6
can be written as

\displaystyle q_{k+1}^{i}=q_{k}^{i}+\alpha_{k}F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1}).

(D.1)

Denote the stationary distribution of the Markov chain
\{S_{k}\}
induced by the joint policy
\pi_{k}=(\pi_{k}^{1},\pi_{k}^{2})
by
\mu_{k}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})
, the existence and uniqueness of which are guaranteed by Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
4.7
(1). Let
\bar{F}_{k}^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}
be defined as

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q^{i})=\mathbb{E}_{S_{0}\sim\mu_{k}(\cdot),A_{0}^{i}\sim\pi_{k}^{i}(\cdot|S_{0}),A_{0}^{-i}\sim\pi_{k}^{-i}(\cdot|S_{0}),S_{1}\sim p(\cdot|S_{0},A_{0}^{i},A_{0}^{-i})}\left[F^{i}(q^{i},S_{0},A_{0}^{i},A_{0}^{-i},S_{1})\right]

for all
q^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{nm_{i}}
.

Lemma D.6
.

The following inequality holds for all
k\geq z_{k}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k+1)]\leq\left(1-\frac{3\mu_{\min}\tau\alpha_{k}}{2m}\right)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)]+\frac{32nm^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\mu_{\min}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}\alpha_{k}}+\frac{84z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
D.6
.
Using the binomial theorem and the equivalent formulation of the update equation in (
D.1
), we have for all
k\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-q_{k}^{i}+q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}+\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\alpha_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})^{\top}\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})^{\top}F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}[(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})^{\top}(\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i})]

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\mu_{\min}\bar{\epsilon}\alpha_{k}}{2m}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+2\alpha_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\frac{4m\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]}{\mu_{\min}\bar{\epsilon}\alpha_{k}}

\displaystyle+2\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})],

(D.2)

where the last line follows from Lemma
4.8
, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, and
a^{2}/c+b^{2}c\geq 2ab
for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
c>0
.

Next, we bound the terms
\mathbb{E}[\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{2}^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]
, and
\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]
in the following lemma, which is a restatement of Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4
.

Lemma D.7
.

The following inequalities hold:

1.

\|F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
for all
k\geq 0
.

2.

\|\bar{q}_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4nm\beta_{k}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
for all
k\geq 0
.

3.

\mathbb{E}[(F^{i}(q_{k}^{i},S_{k},A_{k}^{i},A_{k}^{-i},S_{k+1})-\bar{F}_{k}^{i}(q_{k}^{i}))^{\top}(q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i})]\leq\frac{17z_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
for all
k\geq z_{k}
.

Using Lemma
D.7
in (
D.2
), and recalling that
\bar{\epsilon}=\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k+1}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k+1}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\mu_{\min}\tau\alpha_{k}}{2m}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{8\alpha_{k}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{16nm^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\mu_{\min}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}\alpha_{k}}+\frac{34z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3\mu_{\min}\tau\alpha_{k}}{2m}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{i}-\bar{q}_{k}^{i}\|_{2}^{2}]+\frac{16nm^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\mu_{\min}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}\alpha_{k}}

\displaystyle+\frac{42z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where the last inequality follows from
z_{k}\geq 1
and
\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}\geq\alpha_{k}
, which imply
\alpha_{k}^{2}\leq z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}
. Summing the previous inequality over
i\in\{1,2\}
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k+1)]\leq\left(1-\frac{3\mu_{\min}\tau\alpha_{k}}{2m}\right)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(k)]+\frac{32nm^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}}{\mu_{\min}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}\alpha_{k}}+\frac{84z_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k-z_{k},k-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎∎

D.5  Solving Coupled Lyapunov Drift Inequalities

We first restate the Lyapunov drift inequalities for the value functions, the
q
-functions, and the policies.

•

Lemma
D.3
:
It holds for all
t\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)+4\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)+6\tau\log(m).

(D.3)

•

Lemma
D.4
:
It holds for all
t\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t+1)\leq\gamma\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K).

(D.4)

•

Lemma
D.5
:
It holds for all
t,k\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]+4\beta\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+c_{\pi}\beta\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,k)]

\displaystyle+\frac{12m\tau\beta}{1-\gamma}+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta^{2},

(D.5)

where
c_{\pi}=\frac{8m}{(1-\gamma)\tau}
and
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\cdot]
denotes the conditional expectation given the history up to the beginning of the
t
-th outer loop.

•

Lemma
D.6
:
It holds for all
t\geq 0
and
k\geq z_{\beta}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k+1)]\leq\left(1-c_{q,1}\alpha\right)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]+c_{q,2}\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha}+c_{q,3}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha^{2},

(D.6)

where
c_{q,1}=\frac{3\mu_{\min}\tau}{2m}
,
c_{q,2}=\frac{32nm^{2}}{\mu_{\min}\tau(1-\gamma)^{2}}
, and
c_{q,3}=\frac{84}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

Iterating (
D.6
), we have for all
k\geq z_{\beta}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{q}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-c_{q,1}\alpha)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}+\frac{c_{q,2}\beta^{2}}{c_{q,1}\alpha^{2}}+\frac{c_{q,3}z_{\beta}^{2}\alpha}{c_{q,1}},

(D.7)

where
\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}=\frac{8nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
. Using the previous inequality in (
D.5
), and using
\beta/\alpha\leq c_{q,1}/2
, which implies
(1-c_{q,1}\alpha)^{1/2}\leq 1-\beta
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]+4\beta\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+\frac{12m\tau\beta}{1-\gamma}+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta^{2}

\displaystyle+c_{\pi}\beta\left[(1-\beta)^{k}\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}+\frac{c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha}+\frac{c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}}\right].

Iterating the previous inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,k)]\leq\,

\displaystyle(1-\beta)^{k}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\max}+c_{\pi}\beta k\mathcal{L}^{1/2}_{q,\max})+4\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+\frac{c_{\pi}c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha}+\frac{c_{\pi}c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{12m\tau}{1-\gamma}+2\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta.

(D.8)

Similarly, using (
D.7
) in (
D.4
), and using
(1-c_{q,1}\alpha)^{1/2}\leq 1-\beta
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)+2(1-\beta)^{K}\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}+\frac{2c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha}+\frac{2c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}}.

Taking total expectation on both sides and iterating, since
\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(0)\leq 2/(1-\gamma)
by Lemma
D.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma^{t}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{2\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}}{1-\gamma}(1-\beta)^{K}+\frac{2c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)\alpha}+\frac{2c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)}.

(D.9)

Using (
D.9
), (
D.8
), and (
D.7
) in (
D.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t+1)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+4\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(t)]+2\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{q}^{1/2}(t,K)]+4\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(t,K)]+6\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+\frac{40\gamma^{t}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{40\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}}{1-\gamma}(1-\beta)^{K}+\frac{40c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{40c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)}+4(1-\beta)^{K}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\max}+c_{\pi}\beta K\mathcal{L}^{1/2}_{q,\max})

\displaystyle+\frac{4c_{\pi}c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha}+\frac{4c_{\pi}c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}}+\frac{48m\tau}{1-\gamma}+8\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta

\displaystyle+2(1-\beta)^{K}\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}+\frac{2c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha}+\frac{2c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}}+6\tau\log(m)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]+\frac{40\gamma^{t}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{7c_{\pi}c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha}+\frac{7c_{\pi}c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle+(1-\beta)^{K}\left(4\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\max}+4c_{\pi}\beta K\mathcal{L}^{1/2}_{q,\max}+\frac{42\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}}{1-\gamma}\right)+\frac{54m\tau}{1-\gamma}+8\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta,

where the last inequality follows from
c_{\pi}\geq 16/(1-\gamma)^{2}
. Iterating the previous inequality and using
\mathcal{L}_{v}(0)\leq 4/(1-\gamma)
by Lemma
D.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}_{v}(t)]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4\gamma^{t}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{40t\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{7c_{\pi}c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha(1-\gamma)}+\frac{7c_{\pi}c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}(1-\beta)^{K}\left(4\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\max}+4c_{\pi}\beta K\mathcal{L}^{1/2}_{q,\max}+\frac{42\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}^{1/2}}{1-\gamma}\right)+\frac{54m\tau}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{8\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4\gamma^{t}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{40t\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{7c_{\pi}c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha(1-\gamma)}+\frac{7c_{\pi}c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle+\frac{175n^{1/2}m^{3/2}K}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\tau}(1-\beta)^{K}+\frac{54m\tau}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{8\bar{L}_{\tau}\beta}{1-\gamma},

(D.10)

where the last line follows from

\mathcal{L}_{q,\max}\leq\frac{8nm}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
and

\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\max}\leq\frac{4}{1-\gamma}+2\tau\log(m)
.

Using (
D.7
), (
D.9
), and (
D.10
) together with Lemma
D.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{T,K}^{1},\pi_{T,K}^{2})]\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}\left[2\mathcal{L}_{\text{sum}}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{v}(T)+\mathcal{L}_{\pi}(T,K)+2\tau\log(m)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\frac{T\gamma^{T-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{c_{\pi}c_{q,2}^{1/2}\beta}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}\alpha(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{c_{\pi}c_{q,3}^{1/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{c_{q,1}^{1/2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{n^{1/2}m^{3/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}\tau}K(1-\beta)^{K}+\frac{m\tau}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+\frac{m^{2}\beta}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\frac{T\gamma^{T-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{n^{1/2}m^{5/2}}{\mu_{\min}\tau^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}+\frac{m^{3/2}z_{\beta}\alpha^{1/2}}{\mu_{\min}^{1/2}\tau^{3/2}(1-\gamma)^{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{n^{1/2}m^{3/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}\tau}K(1-\beta)^{K}+\frac{m\tau}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}+\frac{m^{2}\beta}{\tau(1-\gamma)^{4}},

where the last line follows from the definitions of
c_{q,1}
,
c_{q,2}
,
c_{q,3}
, and
c_{\pi}
.

In view of the previous inequality, to achieve
\mathbb{E}[\text{NG}(\pi_{T,K}^{1},\pi_{T,K}^{2})]\leq\epsilon
, it is sufficient to choose
T=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1)
,
\tau=\Theta(\epsilon)
,
\alpha=\tilde{\Theta}(\epsilon^{5})
, and
\beta=\tilde{\Theta}(\epsilon^{8})
, and then take
K=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-8})
. Under this choice, all terms in the preceding bound are at most order
\epsilon
. It follows that the total sample complexity is
TK=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-8})
.

D.6  Proof of Corollary
3.6

The following proof idea was previously used in
[
46
]
to show the rationality of their decentralized
Q
-learning algorithm.

Observe that Theorem
3.5
can be generalized to the case where the reward is corrupted by noise. Specifically, suppose that player
i
takes action
a^{i}
and player
-i
takes action
a^{-i}
. Instead of assuming player
i
receives a deterministic reward
R_{i}(s,a^{i},a^{-i})
, we assume that player
i
receives a random reward
r^{i}(s,a^{i},a^{-i},\xi)
, where
\xi\in\Xi
is a random variable with distribution
\mu_{\xi}(s)
and is independent of everything else. The proof is identical as long as
r^{i}+r^{-i}=0
and the reward is uniformly bounded, i.e.,
\max_{s,a^{i},a^{-i},\xi}|r^{i}(s,a^{i},a^{-i},\xi)|<\infty
.

Now consider the case where player
i
’s opponent follows a stationary policy
\pi^{-i}
. We incorporate the randomness of player
-i
’s action into the model and introduce a fictitious opponent with only one action
a^{*}
. In particular, let the random reward function be defined as
\hat{r}^{i}(s,a^{i},a^{*},A^{-i})=R_{i}(s,a^{i},A^{-i})
for all
(s,a^{i})
, where
A^{-i}\sim\pi^{-i}(\cdot\mid s)
, and let
\hat{p}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a^{i},a^{*})=\sum_{a^{-i}\in\mathcal{A}^{-i}}\pi^{-i}(a^{-i}\mid s)p(s^{\prime}\mid s,a^{i},a^{-i})
. Now the problem can be reformulated as player
i
playing against the fictitious player with a single action
a^{*}
, with reward function
\hat{r}^{i}
and transition probabilities
\hat{p}
. Using the same proof for Theorem
3.5
, we obtain the desired finite-sample bound.

D.7  Statements and Proofs of Supporting Lemmas

Recall that
\Pi_{\bar{\epsilon}}=\{(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\mid\min_{a^{1}}\mu^{1}(a^{1})\geq\bar{\epsilon}/m,\min_{a^{2}}\mu^{2}(a^{2})\geq\bar{\epsilon}/m\}
.

Lemma D.8
.

The function
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
has the following properties.

1.

V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
is
L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}
– smooth on
\Pi_{\bar{\epsilon}}
, where

L_{\tau,\bar{\epsilon}}=2\left(\frac{m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\frac{\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2}^{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2}^{2})}{\tau}+\|X_{1}+X_{2}^{\top}\|_{2}\right)
.

2.

It holds for any
(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{1})\times\Delta(\mathcal{A}^{2})
that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\pi^{1},\pi^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\pi^{2})-\pi^{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\pi^{1},\pi^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\pi^{1})-\pi^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle-V_{X}(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})+4\max_{a^{1},a^{2}}\left|\sum_{i=1,2}X_{i}(a^{i},a^{-i})\right|.

3.

For any
q^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, we have for all
(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})\in\Pi_{\bar{\epsilon}}
that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\pi^{1},\pi^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\pi^{2})\rangle+\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\pi^{1},\pi^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\pi^{1})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 4\bar{\epsilon}\left(\frac{2m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\sum_{i=1,2}\|X_{i}\|_{2}\right)+\frac{2}{\tau}\left(\frac{m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\max(\|X_{1}\|_{2},\|X_{2}\|_{2})\right)\sum_{i=1,2}\|q^{i}-X_{i}\pi^{-i}\|_{2}.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
D.8
.
The proofs of Lemma
D.8
(1) and (2) are identical to those of Lemma
4.5
(1) and (2), and therefore are omitted. Here, we prove Lemma
D.8
(3).

For any
q^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and
q^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
, using the formula of the gradient of
V_{X}(\cdot,\cdot)
from (
C.11
) and the first-order optimality condition of the softmax map in inner-product form, we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{1}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\tau\langle\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2})^{\top}X_{2}(\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\tau\|\nabla\nu(\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2}))-\nabla\nu(\mu^{1})\|_{2}\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\|\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})-\mu^{2}\|_{2}\|X_{2}\|_{2}\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\|X_{2}\|_{2}\right)\left(\|\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})\|_{2}+\|\sigma_{\tau}(q^{1})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{1}\mu^{2})\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\|X_{2}\|_{2}\right)\left(2\bar{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\tau}\|q^{1}-X_{1}\mu^{2}\|_{2}\right),

where the second last inequality follows from the
m/\bar{\epsilon}
– smoothness of
\nu(\cdot)
on
\Pi_{\bar{\epsilon}}
and the fact that the softmax function is
\frac{1}{\tau}
– Lipschitz continuous with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}

[
27
]
, and the last inequality follows from Lemma
B.5
. Similarly, for any
q^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{2}V_{X}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),\sigma_{\tau}^{\bar{\epsilon}}(q^{2})-\sigma_{\tau}(X_{2}\mu^{1})\rangle\leq 2\left(\frac{m\tau}{\bar{\epsilon}}+\|X_{1}\|_{2}\right)\left(2\bar{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\tau}\|q^{2}-X_{2}\mu^{1}\|_{2}\right).

The result follows by adding the previous two inequalities.
∎∎

Appendix E
Numerical Simulations

We conduct numerical simulations to investigate the impact of choosing different
\tau
, which is used to define the softmax operator in Algorithms
1
and
3
. Our theoretical results indicate that there is an asymptotically non-vanishing bias due to using a positive
\tau
. Intuitively, since a softmax policy always has strictly positive entries while a Nash equilibrium policy can have zero entries, we cannot, in general, expect the Nash gap to converge to zero. To demonstrate this phenomenon, consider the following example of a zero-sum matrix game. Let

\displaystyle R_{1}=\begin{bmatrix}N&1&-1\\
-1&0&1\\
1&-1&0\end{bmatrix}

be the payoff matrix for player
1
, and let
R_{2}=-(R_{1})^{\top}
, where
N>0
is a tunable parameter. Note that this matrix game has a unique Nash equilibrium, which goes to the joint policy
\pi^{1}=(1/3,2/3,0)
,
\pi^{2}=(0,2/3,1/3)
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. In our simulations, we use constant stepsizes
\alpha_{k}\equiv 0.5
and
\beta_{k}\equiv 0.01
and run Algorithm
1
for
100
trajectories (each has
K=2000
iterations). Then, we plot the average Nash gap (averaged over the
100
trajectories) as a function of the number of iterations
k
in Figure
1
for different temperatures
\tau
. To enable a fair comparison, we use the normalized
q
-function to compute the softmax, that is, instead of directly using
\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i})
in Algorithm
1
, we use
\sigma_{\tau}(q_{k}^{i}/\|q_{k}^{i}\|_{2})
. As we can see in Figure
1
, as
\tau
increases, the asymptotic error also increases, which is consistent with our theoretical results.

Figure 1:
The Nash Gap for Different Temperatures
\tau
